# DRISHTI-PGK — Design Criteria Register

**Problem statement:** SIH26098 — *Development of a Low-Cost Precision Guidance and Smart Electronic Fuze System for a 155 mm Artillery Shell*
**Organisation:** Ministry of Defence (MoD) / Yantra India Limited, Ambajhari, Nagpur
**Purpose of this file:** the single working record of every criterion the design must satisfy or decide. It is the evidence base for the submission — every number that appears in the submission should trace back to a row here.

---

## How to use this file

**Every criterion carries one of three types:**

| Type | Meaning | What you do |
|---|---|---|
| **Given** | Imposed by the customer, the platform, or a defence standard | Extract it. Do not invent it. |
| **Derived** | Falls out of physics once the givens are fixed | Compute it. Show the arithmetic and the assumptions. |
| **Chosen** | A design decision of ours | Decide it, and justify it against the givens with a trade. |

**Status values — keep them honest:**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `OPEN` | Not yet defined |
| `OPEN-BLOCKED` | *(retired 2026-09-17 — superseded by `PARAMETRIC`)* Cannot be fixed until external data arrives. |
| `PARAMETRIC` | **Sized over a declared envelope built from public data** (`design/02-reference-155mm-model.md`). Neither blocked nor parked: a bounded design value is in use, with a stated public basis and a sensitivity showing how the design closes across the envelope. Every PARAMETRIC value is re-baselined at P6. |
| `PROPOSED` | A value is proposed but not yet confirmed by the team |
| `DEFINED` | Confirmed by the team |
| `DERIVED` | Computed from other defined criteria |
| `VERIFIED` | Confirmed by analysis, simulation or test |

**Governing criteria are marked ⚑.** These are where the margins are thinnest and where the design will be won or lost. Get these right before anything else.

**Values marked *provisional* are derived on top of a placeholder, an unconfirmed anchor, or a [ref] value.** A provisional value names the input it depends on and never counts as closed, however solid the arithmetic looks.

**Values marked **[ref]** are carried from the reference literature for the 155 mm class (`notes/00-PGK-thesis-working-notes.md`) and must be confirmed against YIL's actual shell.**

---

## The dependency chain

Criteria cannot be defined out of order. This is the sequence:

```
M  Mission (target, range, charge)
     └─ baseline unguided dispersion
         └─ required correction capability
             └─ G  guidance & control requirements
                 └─ C  canard sizing → Taero → alternator sizing → friction budget
                     └─ N  navigation & sensing requirements
                         └─ W  power, thermal
                             └─ S/P/E/F  structure, stability, environment, fuze
                                 └─ K/R  cost, verification
```

S  (Stability) and P (Platform) run in parallel with the chain — they constrain it, and they are gated on the YIL shell data.

## Submission-critical track

These are the criteria whose answers actually change what we can claim in the SIH26098 idea submission — the ones that make the Technical Approach and Feasibility slides specific rather than generic. Everything else matters to the design but only in the later design phase, and is deliberately excluded here.

**This shortlist is the priority order for the remaining interview.** Within each domain, work these criteria before the rest of the domain; the domains themselves still run unblocked-first (G → C → F → N → W → R).

| # | ID | Criterion | Why it matters for the submission |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | M3 | Range band and charge compatibility | "Works across charges 3M–6M without touching the ammunition" is the strongest feasibility sentence available; without it the whole retrofit premise collapses. |
| 2 | M4 | Range at which CEP ≤ 30 m is claimed | A 30 m CEP with no range attached is the first thing a reviewer discounts; naming the max-range / 6M condition makes the headline number testable. |
| 3 | M5 | CEP definition and treatment of target-location error | States the claim in a form we control and publishes the dependency instead of hiding it — pre-empts the standing "your CEP excludes what?" challenge. |
| 4 | M6 | Baseline dispersion anchor (CEP 267 m → σ 227 m) | Quantifies the problem we exist to solve and turns "artillery is inaccurate" into a cited number that every performance slide can lean on. |
| 5 | M7 | Required correction authority (407 m and 19.5 m/s at 80% capture) | The feasibility crux: it shows the required authority is achievable by the chosen architecture, states the margin, and is the head of the entire CAA sizing chain. |
| 6 | M8 | Effectiveness metric (rounds per target / Pk) | Translates CEP into the operator-level payoff — rounds and time per mission — which is what the impact story is actually scored on. |
| 7 | G1 | Guidance law type | The PS asks for a "guidance and control algorithm" and the reference work deliberately has none, so this is our clearest novel contribution. |
| 8 | G3 | Guidance window | Shows authority is a window, not a constant — the most technically credible slide in the deck, and the honest basis for the apogee risk. |
| 9 | G4 | Miss-distance allocation to the guidance loop | The error budget is what makes "CEP ≤ 30 m" an engineering claim rather than an aspiration, and this is its largest term. |
| 10 | G6 | Absolute (earth-referenced) ring-angle accuracy | Direct, unattenuated error into the direction the guidance force points; naming the requirement shows the novel sensing problem has been recognised. |
| 11 | G15 | Limit-scheduling law | The reference work lost control between 30 s and 50 s with fixed limits; inheriting the state-dependent fix is a concrete, hardware-validated differentiator. |
| 12 | C1 | Canard count and arrangement | The architecture figure — steering pair plus control pair, no moving control surfaces — makes "canard actuation assembly" concrete in one slide. |
| 13 | C8 | Net guidance force required | Closes M7 against the hardware: the force the canards must actually generate, steady and transient. |
| 14 | C9 | Torque authority — symmetric criterion | The sizing equation tying canard size to alternator capability, and the equation a technical reviewer will test us on. |
| 15 | C12 | Taero minimum near apogee | The thinnest margin in the design and the reason friction and bearing selection are first-order; stating it pre-empts the deepest technical objection. |
| 16 | C13 | Friction budget | Sets what the bearings must achieve — the controllability criterion collapses entirely if this is not held. |
| 17 | C26 | Ring setback load | The launch-survival number the CAD is checked against: evidence that 15 000 g has been turned into a structural case rather than merely quoted. |
| 18 | C31 | In-bore stowage and safety | The safety question every judge asks of a canard kit — nothing protrudes, nothing fouls the bore or rammer — and it must be answered before a flyable concept can be claimed. |
| 19 | F1 | Fuze modes and setting ranges | **Closed 2026-09-17 — all three PS modes with a full setting envelope:** proximity burst height **2–15 m**, time **0.1–199.9 s** at 0.1 s resolution, impact **superquick / short delay / graze** — **plus an in-flight re-synchronisation of the time setting from the nav solution**, which is what makes the airburst track a *guided* round's actual time of flight. The fuze half of the project now has numbers where it had a statement of intent, and the mode set exceeds the fielded benchmark's PD + proximity. Gates F3, F4, F5 |
| 20 | F2 | Proximity sensor technology | **Closed 2026-09-17 — RF Doppler at 24 GHz class (ISM), radome in the nose tip, no optical window.** The multi-mode fuze is now a design rather than a wish: the frequency is chosen for spectral separation from both the GNSS receiver (1.2/1.5 GHz) and the alternator's ≈2.1 kHz electrical content, which reduces F15 to the converter's switching harmonics. The decision carried out one requirement of its own — **the detector shall reject the spin modulation of the ground return** (176.3–304.4 Hz body spin), because a threshold that ignores rotation bursts at the wrong height |
| 21 | F6 | Safe-and-arm type | **Closed 2026-09-17 — MIL-STD-1316 out-of-line rotor, released by two independent mechanical stimuli (setback 15 000 g and spin 176.3–304.4 Hz) with the electrical arm command as a third, independent condition.** The subsystem a defence reviewer probes first is now a design: the rotor cannot complete its travel while setback is sustained, so **no in-bore function is possible by construction rather than by timer**; dead electronics give a dud, never a premature burst; and a misfire is safe with no abort logic. The two stimuli are quantities the register already owned, so F7 inherits named anchors rather than invented thresholds |
| 22 | F12 | Setter data package contents | **Closed 2026-09-17 — the full mission package through the in-service inductive setter: target coordinates, met, charge / muzzle-velocity state, time reference, GNSS ephemeris and keys, mode and parameters, plus round ID — with a verified read-back, a crew go/no-go and a target sanity check against the firing solution.** This is what makes G1 executable rather than aspirational: the round computes its own solution in flight against the uploaded target instead of following a pre-fire path. It is also the difference between pre-fire programming and a mode word, and it closes the first half of risk 13 |
| 23 | F15 | EMI coexistence | **Closed 2026-09-17 — answered, not merely named.** The two magnetic disturbances are both functions of quantities the kit already measures: the rotating term is at **1 234–2 131 Hz** (7 pole pairs × the measured relative rotation) and is rejected by a **coherent notch**, and the current-proportional term is **compensated against measured coil current** in the in-flight calibration model — which is what G6 actually asked for. The converter's switching spectrum is specified for L-band quiet. The **negative result is as useful as the positive one**: the magnetometer case cannot be solved spatially, because interference and measurand share the same band *and* the same space — a sentence that pre-empts the first question a radio engineer will ask |
| 24 | N1 | Navigation accuracy at impact | **Closed 2026-09-17 — the 5 m allocation now closes from below at RSS 3.7 m** (GNSS 3.0 + IMU and filter 2.0 + time/lever-arm 1.0), a **≈26 % margin**, where before it was a top-down number with nothing under it. Two consequences came out of the derivation and matter more than the figure: the line is **conditional on GNSS availability** — unaided, 0.1° of attitude error costs ≈110 m over 50 s — so N4 owes a bounded outage tolerance; and the IMU term is only achievable because the IMU rides the **de-spun ring** (N6), which is the architectural claim slide 3 makes and this row now underpins |
| 25 | N3 | GNSS antenna strategy under spin | **Closed 2026-09-17 — a rotationally symmetric aperture on the spin axis, elements combined rather than switched, so the spin produces no modulation *by symmetry*.** The hardest sensing problem in the design is answered by geometry rather than by signal processing: no receiver redesign, no tracking-loop work, only the slow coning motion left to absorb. Two consequences came with it and are worth stating on the slide — the aperture now shares the axis with the 24 GHz radar and the radome must pass **both** L1 and 24 GHz (risk 27), and **fast acquisition stops being a performance goal and becomes a requirement**, because G3's opening edge is nav convergence and the window behind it is spent against a 1.11:1 margin. The rejected option is instructive: putting the aperture on the **de-spun ring** is refuted by our own architecture, because the ring angle *is* the guidance command channel (G2) |
| 26 | N6 | IMU on the de-spun ring | **Closed 2026-09-18 — the ring mount is a decided criterion, with the body-side spin channel (N9/N10) that the limit laws and the F15 notch already require (option C).** The measurement is taken with the spin removed *mechanically*: the unit sees the airframe's coning (a few rad/s transverse) plus its own slew about the roll axis (±31.42 rad/s limit), so a standard ±2,000 °/s MEMS qualifies (N7) and a tactical-grade ARW ≤ 1 °/√h holds the aided residual (N8). **It back-fills N1's 2.0 m IMU term** — the navigation budget's second leg now has its mount — and it is the quantitative backing for slide 3's architectural claim. Cost carried: power and data cross the ring–body interface (risk 34). |
| 27 | W4 | Generation capability versus demand | **Closed 2026-09-18 — the budget exists, and it inverts the intuition: the demand never binds.** Demand ≈12 W peak / ≈10 W average (≈0.9 kJ per flight) against **2.92 kW peak / 121 kJ generated** (C42) with a 0.24 kW floor at apogee — **≈20:1 at the worst point**, and the avionics are ≈0.8 % of the energy. The design question is routing: the surplus goes to the dump (W7/W12), and a **supercapacitor buffer** (≈150–200 J, ≈50–150 g) carries the fireset reservoir, ≥2 s of avionics hold-up and the actuator's motoring bursts. "**No in-flight battery**" survives intact — a supercapacitor is not a battery. |
| 28 | W7 | Control energy dissipation path | Unaddressed in the reference work and a coin-flip question at technical review; having an answer distinguishes us from a straight copy. |
| 29 | K4 | Unit cost target at a stated production volume | **Closed 2026-09-18 — ≤ ₹6.5 lakh (≈ US$7,800) recurring unit cost at 5,000 kits/year sustained, published as "the fielded price minus 40 %"** against the M1156's public **US$13,541** unit cost. The adjective now has a number, a volume and a citable anchor — and the obligation to support it at that volume is K5's. |
| 30 | R5 | Monte Carlo definition | **Closed 2026-09-18 — a two-tier campaign: 5,000 runs at the design condition (±2.0 % at 95 % confidence) plus 1,000 at each of five envelope corners, ≈10,000 full-fidelity flights with the guidance loop in the loop.** The claim is supported by the **worst corner's 95 % upper bound**, not the design-point estimate; the two model-error terms are sampled with a stated correlation (settling risk 16's double-count); target-location error is switched separately per M5. The statistical basis is why the spend is on conditions: the sample median's relative SE is ≈ 0.72/√N, so the margin is not statistical — **the coefficient set is the dominant uncertainty.** |
| 31 | R7 | Bench rig capability specification | **Closed 2026-09-18 — the specification is set; the rig is built only if shortlisted, and it is specified to showcase the full capability of the design (user decision).** Capabilities: friction at flight-representative speed (up to ≈ 2 000 rad/s, against the reference's 19.4×-low datum — C13/C25, risk 24); the machine as generator at the real 2.92 kW into the dump and buffer (C42/W7/W12/W4); closed-loop ring-angle tracking with DOB and state-dependent limits, overshoot ≤ 10 % and never past δ (G11, whose provisional δ this rig fixes); magnetometer coexistence at speed (F15); the 24 GHz spin-modulation test (F2/F3); the ring–body interface (risk 34) and a setback pulse on the buffer (risk 35); the fuze-well interface in W/K (P6). **Build gate: P7, post-selection — presented in the submission as a plan, never as an asset.** The items it verifies therefore stay *declared open verifications* rather than demonstrated results. |
| 32 | S1 | Gyroscopic stability factor with the kit installed | **Closed 2026-09-17 — now PARAMETRIC, and it is the strongest stability sentence the submission can carry.** Canards ahead of the CG are destabilising, so S1 is the criterion that can veto the architecture. First-order Sg = **1.37–3.55** across the declared envelope, **design point 1.83**, with a derived **canard allowance of ≤ 5 % ΔC_Mα at the conservative corner**. The deck now claims stability with a stated margin and a stated condition instead of naming a risk. |
| 33 | P9 | Kit mass limit | **Closed 2026-09-17 — now PARAMETRIC.** The fielded benchmark kit is 1.4 kg against a ≈0.98 kg standard fuze, so the net penalty is **≈0.42 kg — 0.9 % of the projectile** — giving a **2.6 mm** net CG shift and **+5.4 %** Ixx. The drop-in retrofit claim now rests on a published number instead of a missing one. |
| 34 | **C42** | Continuous generated power — the energy that must be dissipated | **Added 2026-09-18 after the machine sizing.** The architecture's defining cost: the kit generates **2.92 kW peak and ≈121 kJ per flight** that it must get rid of. Stating this openly — with the trade against a servo-actuated canard design that carries no such load — is worth far more than leaving it out. A submission that has found its own hardest problem reads as competence. |
| 35 | **C43** | Cant-angle ↔ roll-torque ↔ dumped-power coupling | Shows the guidance-force trade is understood as a *system*: raising cant buys force and costs dissipation in equal measure (+60 % for +60 %). Closes the loop between C8, C9 and C42, and corrects the register's earlier treatment of cant as free. |
| 36 | **C40 + C34/C36** | Machine identity and mass | The Technical Approach needs one honest sentence on what the machine is — frameless outrunner, 12 slots / 14 poles, ≈79 mm gap, ≈0.9 kg. That mass is ≈64 % of the entire M1156 benchmark kit. Better stated by us than discovered by a judge. |
| 37 | **C35** | Air gap → bearing stiffness → launch survival | Turns "15 000 g" from a quoted number into a structural case: a 0.40 mm gap means **0.20 mm maximum relative deflection**, which is a *stiffness* specification on the bearing pair — harder than the friction budget it already carried. |
| 38 | **W12** | Dump stage mass, area and location | Where the architecture's 121 kJ physically goes. Unresolved, and it competes with the fuze stack for the same volume (risk 27). This is the item that separates a de-spun ring from a servo-actuated design in the Feasibility argument. |
| 39 | **H1–H13** | Embedded hardware architecture — and the electronics block diagram | **Added 2026-09-18; the PS asks for it twice over.** Requirement **R4** — *"embedded electronics + software for real-time flight control"* — and the **"Electronics block diagram"** deliverable are both named in the PS's own list, and until now no criterion owned either; risk 19 said it outright ("no criterion in the register owns the flight computer"). The block owns the compute split (H1, amending G13's mechanism — fabric → dedicated core — with risk 36 stating the price), the flight-software architecture and the ground/flight builds (H2, which is where R5's ≈10,000-flight campaign runs), memory and the compute budget (H3/H4, **closing risk 19**), the board partition and launch survival (H5), the sensor chain and time base (H6, **closing N15**), the ring–body contactless link (H7, **giving risk 34 the owner it lacked**), the machine and dump power electronics (H8/H9), the fuze and setter interfaces (H10/H11), the rails (H12, **closing W8**) and BIT (H13, **closing R3**). Its artifact is the single-slide block diagram, every block labelled with the criterion that puts it there — a named deliverable produced in the same pass as the design work |

**Every entry on this track is now unblocked (2026-09-17).** M3, M4, M6, S1 and P9 moved from `OPEN-BLOCKED`/parked to `PARAMETRIC` with declared values and a sensitivity, on the reference model (`design/02-reference-155mm-model.md`). No criterion on the track is waiting on YIL data.

**Growth module (X domain) — now bounded, and still not in the baseline.** **X1 was decided 2026-09-18: the seeker is an optional growth module**, described in the deck as a bounded option with a defined interface (X18), never as a delivered capability. Three of the four criteria that change what the submission may claim are now closed — **X3** (the geometric terminal window: no target-referenced guidance exists anywhere in the ascent; the window opens in the descent and is FOV-limited, **≈20 s at a 20° aperture**), **X4** (correctable miss **≈59 m** at the design point, bounded above by 89 m — **this supersedes the register's old "≈35–90 m" pair, which mixed two guidance gains and must not be quoted**), and **X5** (the derived bound worth putting on the slide: **85 % of the 407 m requirement still has to come from GNSS/INS**, so a seeker narrows the GPS-denied error rather than closing it). **The fourth — X17 — was decided in the same session: the primary GPS-denied answer is anti-jam GNSS with INS coasting** (the fielded analogue's answer, in the same envelope and the same cost class, and riding N3's multi-element spin-axis aperture rather than adding an antenna), **with the seeker no more than the secondary.** With all four settled, the deck may carry the growth-module slide — bounded by X5's **59 m of 407 m** and by X16's cost delta, and never describing the module as drop-in. X2 and X7–X19 remain open and none of them may be quoted as a capability.

**Excluded from this track** — criteria that only matter in the later design phase: the sub-system selections that follow once the governing numbers are fixed (C2, C3, C5–C7, C10, C14–C25, C27, C28, C30, C32, C33), the qualification detail behind the standards we simply cite (E2–E11), the component-level sensing and power choices (N2, N4, N5, N7–N11, N13–N15, W2, W3, W5, W6, W8–W11), and the drawing-dependent platform rows (P1–P8, P12–P14).

## M — Mission & effectiveness

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | Target set (point / area, personnel, materiel, hardened) | Given | Mixed general-purpose: point / light materiel primary, personnel and soft targets secondary (proximity) | PS silent on target set — user requirement, confirmed 2026-09-17 | DEFINED |
| M2 | Target hardness and required terminal effect | Given | Full effect envelope retained: proximity airburst, time airburst, impact with selectable superquick / short delay / graze — all modes configurable before flight; the fuze shall not reduce the terminal-effect envelope of the fuze it replaces | PS (three-mode fuze) + user requirement 2026-09-17. Envelope option B selected on the user's instruction ("decide on one which satisfies it") — the selection is the assistant's recommendation, not the user's instruction | DEFINED |
| M3 | Range band (min / max) and charge(s) (3M–6M; MV 546–943 m/s) | Given | **PARAMETRIC — sized over the declared envelope.** Full functional envelope across charges 3M–6M: safe, stable and functional at every charge. Declared velocity envelope **546–943 m/s**, which the public charge-compatibility record covers conservatively (the M795 is qualified for 3W–8S, M3A1–M203A1). Range band: the reference class reaches **22.5 km (M795) to 30.1 km (M549A1)** at maximum charge from a 39-calibre tube; the claim range is set at M4. CEP ≤ 30 m claimed only over that bounded band; outside it accuracy degrades but never below unguided | PS + user requirement 2026-09-17 (option C); charge/velocity envelope from the thesis **[ref]**; range class from `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §2 (public M795 / M549A1 data) | PARAMETRIC |
| M4 | Reference range at which "CEP ≤ 30 m" is claimed | Chosen | **PARAMETRIC — numeric range read from the reference class.** Maximum-range, full-charge (6M) engagement. The reference class brackets this at **22.5 km (M795, 39 cal) to 30.1 km (M549A1, 39 cal)**; the **design case is the 30 km class**, because dispersion is worst there (267 m CEP against 139 m at 22.5 km) and the guidance window is longest. Mid-band accuracy reported as supporting data from the same Monte Carlo campaign, not as a formal second claim | PS states 30 m without a range; user choice 2026-09-17 (option A); range class from `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §2 | PARAMETRIC |
| M5 | CEP definition: 50% probability radius; is target-location error inside or outside the budget? | Chosen | CEP = 2-D circular 50% probability radius (CEP = 1.1774σ for a circular normal distribution). **Formal claim: weapon CEP against the loaded aimpoint, target-location error excluded and stated as excluded.** End-to-end CEP against the true target published as a sensitivity with the assumed target-location error named. **Published form (added 2026-09-19): the claim is stated as CEP *and* the fraction of rounds within 30 m** — the requirement is a 30 m figure, so quoting only a median that sits 20 % inside it hides the tail that decides whether the requirement is met. Both numbers come from the same R5 campaign; target-location error stays outside the formal claim per the sentence above | PS states 30 m; user choice 2026-09-17 (option C) | DEFINED |
| M6 | ⚑ Baseline unguided dispersion at M3/M4 | Given | **Cited anchor now in use: CEP = 267 m at maximum range for a conventional unguided M549A1 155 mm projectile → equivalent circular 1σ = CEP / 1.1774 = 227 m.** The 267 m applies at the M549A1's maximum range (30.1 km from the M198 howitzer) — the same order as the M4 condition, but it is **not** YIL's round and **not** YIL's range. **The range/deflection split is not yet known:** the source publishes only the circular CEP, so the ratio of range-direction to deflection dispersion must still come from YIL. The CEP form does remove the 1σ-versus-peak-to-peak ambiguity | **Three public citations bracket the value:** M549A1 **CEP 267 m** at 30.1 km (the fielded-kit literature); M549 **CEP 259 m** at maximum range, 39 cal (NDIA Precision Strike Annual Review 2012); M795 **CEP 139 m** at 22.5 km, 39 cal (same source). σ = 267 / 1.1774 = 226.8 m. Dispersion is strongly range-dependent, so the anchor is tied to M4's maximum-range condition and the **227 m end governs**. YIL firing tables (post-selection item 3) confirm or replace — **sizes everything downstream** | PARAMETRIC — cited anchor, not an assumption |
| M7 | ⚑ Required correction capability (impact correction in m; equivalent Δv and burn time) | Derived | **Provisional — rests on the M6 anchor and on [ref] platform values.** Restated as a **capture-fraction** problem, not a percentile lookup. For a Rayleigh radial distribution with σ = 227 m, the authority needed to capture a fraction P of rounds is A = σ·√(−2·ln(1−P)): P = 50% → 267 m (the CEP-defining population — buys nothing better than unguided); P = 70% → 352 m; **P = 80% → 407 m — chosen**; P = 85% → 442 m; P = 90% → 487 m, beyond the authority available. With the reference-model design mass **m = 45.0 kg** and F = 20 N: a = F/m = 0.444 m/s² → **A = 407 m → full-force burn T = √(2A/a) = 42.8 s, equivalent Δv = aT = 19.0 m/s.** Authority available from a 20 N force across the ≈45 s terminal window (apogee t ≈ 41 s → impact t ≈ 86 s) is **450 m / 19.0 m/s → margin ≈ 1.11:1**. *(Re-cut 2026-09-17: the original derivation used the `[ref]` 43 kg mass and gave 41.8 s / 19.5 m/s / 471 m / 1.16:1. The mass re-baseline costs 4 % of the authority margin. Cross-checked by C8, which inverts the same relation into a force requirement: F = 2·A·m/T² ≥ 18.1 N.)* **Split:** a single 2-D force vector serves both axes, so the components combine as A = √(A_range² + A_def²); the split is unknown pending M6. **Range correction costs more authority than deflection and the two are not interchangeable** — a range correction is trajectory-mediated, must be spent while the force is fighting the velocity vector, and changes time of flight; a deflection correction is a near-pure terminal displacement integral that can be spent late | Derivation: A = σ·√(−2·ln(1−P)) with σ = 227 m from M6; a = F/m with F = 20 N **[ref]** and **m = 45.0 kg** (reference-model design point, superseding the `[ref]` 43 kg); T = √(2A/a), Δv = aT; terminal window ≈ 45 s from apogee t ≈ 41 s to impact t ≈ 86 s **[ref]**. From M6 − M5; the force form is cross-checked by C8, which inverts it | DERIVED — provisional (M6 anchor + reference-model mass) |
| M8 | Effectiveness metric (rounds per target, Pk) | Derived | **DERIVED — provisional (M6 anchor + r_eff assumption).** Metric: rounds for a **90 % probability of target defeat** against the primary target class (point / light materiel), effective radius **r_eff = 30 m**, independent rounds at one aimpoint. Guided — σ = 25.5 m (claimed CEP 30 m ÷ 1.1774): P₁ = 50 % → **4 rounds**; unguided — σ = 227 m (M6 anchor): P₁ = 0.87 % → **≈264 rounds** → **≈66× reduction**. Four-round mission: 94 % guided vs 3.4 % unguided. Published sensitivity: r_eff 20 m → 8 vs 592; 50 m → 2 vs 95; design point (CEP ≈ 24 m) → 3 rounds. r_eff is a lethality assumption — YIL terminal-effects data or standard lethality tables verify it | From M5 (circular-normal form) and M6 (anchor): P₁ = 1 − exp(−r²/2σ²), n = ln(0.1)/ln(1−P₁); Decision Log 2026-09-17 | DERIVED — provisional (M6 anchor + r_eff assumption) |
| M9 | Gun platform(s) and calibre length (39 / 45 / 52 cal) | Given | | YIL / user | OPEN |
| M10 | **Range retention: the fraction of the conventional round's maximum range the kit must preserve** | Chosen | **Claimed — the kit shall retain >90 % of the conventional all-up round's maximum-range capability**, stated as a system-level requirement rather than left implicit. The anchor is the fielded kit's own published claim: *"Maintains >90 % of range capability of conventional all up round"* (Orbital ATK, PGK program update, NDIA Armament Systems Forum 2015). **Why it needed a row:** the kit is specified as a drop-in retrofit that must not change the shell, the charge system or the gun, so whatever range it costs, it costs through the canards' drag and the kit's own 1.4–1.7 kg — and **no criterion owned that exchange rate**. Canard sizing (C4/C5) and cant (C43) were free to buy accuracy at the range's expense with nothing checking the trade. M10 closes that: a correction purchased with more than 10 % of the range has to be justified against this row, not against C8 alone | Public fielded requirement: Orbital ATK, *Precision Guidance Kit (PGK) Program Update*, NDIA 2015 — *"Maintains >90 % of range capability of conventional all up round"*. Source register A1. Sizes C4/C5/C43; bounds the authority window M7 assumes | **DEFINED — the requirement is set at the fielded class figure; the achieved retention is derived at P3** |

## P — Platform compatibility  *(all PARAMETRIC on the reference model — see `02-reference-155mm-model.md`)*

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Shell model(s) and drawing/datasheet reference | Given | **Any fuzed 155 mm HE projectile with a MIL-STD-333B nose fuze well**, in the M795 / M549A1 class — the two platforms the fielded M1156 PGK is qualified on. The kit is specified as a **retrofit against the interface standard**, not against one shell | MIL-STD-333B; DOT&E PGK FY2015; `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §2, §4 | PARAMETRIC |
| P2 | Shell mass | Given | **45.0 kg design point; envelope 43–47 kg** (M549A1 ≈44.6 kg fuzed with a 975 g fuze; M795 46.7 kg as fired) | Public manufacturer datasheets and Jane's-derived records — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §2 | PARAMETRIC |
| P3 | Shell CG location | Given | **0.585 L from the nose at the design point (0.50 m); envelope 0.55–0.62 L** (0.46–0.53 m) | Derived from public geometry, calibrated on the published ERFB/BB CAD mass-property set — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §3 | PARAMETRIC |
| P4 | Shell axial inertia Ixx | Given | **0.155 kg·m² design point; envelope 0.144–0.167 kg·m²** | Derived, kx-calibrated (kx = 1.401). Cross-checks against the thesis `[ref]` 0.144 at 43 kg within 5 % — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §3 | PARAMETRIC |
| P5 | Shell transverse inertia Iyy = Izz | Given | **1.75 kg·m² design point; envelope 1.22–1.81 kg·m².** **The thesis `[ref]` value of 1.216 is deliberately NOT adopted as the design case** — two independent public routes give 1.66–1.81, and a low Iyy flatters Sg, which is the criterion that can veto the architecture. The envelope spans both so the design closes at the conservative end | Derived, ky-calibrated (ky = 0.929), cross-projected from the published ERFB/BB set — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §3. Divergence logged as risk 21 | PARAMETRIC |
| P6 | Fuze well thread form, depth, sealing, torque spec | Given | **MIL-STD-333B** standardised nose fuze thread / contour / cavity; **thread 2″-12 UNS-2A**; standard fuze height above the seat 151.6 mm max; sealing and torque spec to be confirmed against YIL's drawing. **EXTENDED 2026-09-18 — this interface is now a THERMAL criterion as well as a mechanical one** (W7/W12): it must carry **2.92 kW peak / ≈121 kJ per flight** into the shell, and **the bare joint does not do that.** First-order estimate: **flank + seat ≈ 3–16 W/K, central ≈ 8 W/K** — thread flank 1.12e-3 m² (≈9 turns × π·d_p·b, b ≈ 0.8 mm effective band) at h_c 2–10 kW/(m²·K) → 2–11 W/K; seat 0.5e-3 m² (Ø44–50.8 annulus) → 1–5 W/K. Against W7's **≥ 58 W/K** peak figure that is **≈4–19× short (central ≈ 7×)**; against the flight-average requirement — 121 kJ/90 s = 1.34 kW, ≈1.0 kW net of the airstream → **≥ 20–27 W/K** — it is **≈1.5–3× short**. **What closes the gap, in order of leverage: (1) AREA, not surface quality — the well bore**, π·Ø48 mm × 124.7 mm ≈ **18.8e-3 m², ≈17× the flank area**; a *contacting* radial path there (compliant conductive sleeve, or light interference) gives **19–94 W/K** at h_c 1–5 kW/(m²·K) — and a plain clearance fit does **not** count, because a 0.1 mm air film over that same area is only ≈5 W/K; **(2) raise h_c at the joint** — ground/lapped flanks and seat plus a controlled torque spec (h_c ≈ P^0.95, so **the torque spec is now a thermal requirement too**), ≈×2 at doubled preload; **(3) split peak from average** — the 2.92 kW launch transient is buffered by the kit's own metal (≈400–450 J/K → ≈+40 K), so the interface is sized on the 90 s average; **(4) verify by test** — a P7/R7 rig item (representative joint + heater + ΔT), not an analysis item. **Derived consequence: the 45 kg shell is a long-time sink, not an immediate one** — the thermal penetration depth in steel over the 90 s flight is **≈30–65 mm**, so the flight-time participating mass is the kit plus the shell's nose (**≈6–8 kg → ≈25–35 K rise**) and W7's **5.8 K** is the asymptote, reached in minutes. **So the interface must be designed as an interface — a stated contact area and a stated contact pressure — not inherited from a structural thread.** **Engagement length is a thermal lever, not only a structural one (added 2026-09-19, from the project's predecessor study).** The 19 mm engagement assumed above is a class *minimum*, not a design limit: the thread can be cut to the **full well cavity depth**, and the predecessor CAD in this project did exactly that — **≈90 mm of engagement (≈42 turns at 12 tpi, 50 N·m install torque) against the STANAG-class ≈25 mm practice**, chosen there on structural grounds. The flank term is linear in engaged length, so the same move here takes the bare-joint estimate from **≈3–16 W/K (central ≈8) to ≈12–57 W/K (central ≈29)** — the flank contribution alone (2–11 → ≈10–52 W/K) then spans the **≥ 20–27 W/K flight-average requirement on its own**, and the peak gap narrows from ≈7× to ≈1–5×. Engagement length therefore joins the well bore as a closing lever, at no mass or volume cost, bounded by the well drawing, by the thread's structural duty (C26, E1) and by keeping the joint a *conductance-specified* one (route 2 above). **Consequence for the re-baseline:** engaged length and the torque spec are no longer merely drawing items to read off — they are the two variables that set this interface, and post-selection document 2 must be asked for both. **Thread callout to reconcile:** this register writes **2″-12 UNS-2A** (from MIL-STD-333B); the predecessor CAD writes **2-12UN-1A / 2″-12 UNEF-2A** for the same diameter and pitch (2.000 in × 12 tpi) under a different series/class callout. Same joint, three callouts — take the series and class from the drawing at P6; nothing downstream depends on which is right. | MIL-STD-333B (1989); M557 fuze datasheet — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §4. Thermal requirement from W7/W12 (C42); conductance estimated 2026-09-18 with h_c bracketed 2–10 kW/(m²·K) (machined steel–steel, air-filled gaps, 1–20 MPa preload from an assumed 100 N·m torque spec, h_c ∝ P^0.95), engagement assumed 19 mm. Bore diameter, seat annulus, engaged thread length and the torque spec are **drawing items — post-selection document 2** | PARAMETRIC — **extended 2026-09-18: a thermal criterion now; ≥ 58 W/K does NOT close on the thread alone — the closing route is the well bore + a conductance-specified joint, verified by test** |
| P7 | Available axial envelope forward of fuze well | Given | **≥ 124.7 mm available intrusion** — the depth the fielded M1156 PGK uses on both reference platforms. Kit design target ≤ 110 mm, leaving margin | Public M1156 PGK dimensions — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §4 | PARAMETRIC |
| P8 | Ogive contour — maximum kit diameter at each station | Given | **Tangent-ogive model through the published nose length** (nose 2.4129 cal of a 4.5097 cal body), truncated at Ø50.8 mm. Shell diameter is **97.9 mm at the forward end of the kit's 95 mm visible length**, reaching 154.9 mm 370 mm aft of the seat. Full table in `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §5 | Derived from published M107-class geometry; method caveat recorded | PARAMETRIC |
| P9 | ⚑ Kit mass limit | Given / Chosen | **≤ 1.4 kg total and ≤ 0.5 kg net of the fuze it replaces** (the fielded benchmark kit is 1.4 kg against a ≈0.98 kg standard fuze). Net platform effect at the design point: **2.6 mm CG shift forward, 0.31 % of projectile length** | M1156 PGK public mass; net effect derived — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §4, §8 | PARAMETRIC |
| P10 | ⚑ Kit CG shift limit | Given / Chosen | **≤ 0.31 % of projectile length forward (2.6 mm)** net of the replaced fuze at the design point; **11.5 mm** if the kit is treated as pure added mass. Obligation: hold this or better, and demonstrate it against S1 | Derived from the reference model §8 | PARAMETRIC |
| P11 | ⚑ ΔIxx limit | Given / Chosen | **ΔIxx ≤ +5.4 %** with the ring and canards at body radius, or **≤ +0.4 %** for in-well electronics alone. **This is stability-positive**: +5.4 % of Ixx raises Sg by ≈11 %, partly offsetting the canards' destabilising effect | Derived from the reference model §8 | PARAMETRIC |
| P12 | Kit overall envelope (length × diameter) | Derived | **Ø50.8 mm (well thread) × ≤124.7 mm intrusion + ≤95.25 mm visible length.** The fielded benchmark totals 220.2 mm overall (124.7 + 95.25 = 219.95 ✓) | Public M1156 PGK dimensions; derived from P7, P8 | PARAMETRIC |
| P13 | Driving band / rifling interface (unchanged by design) | Given | **Untouched by construction.** The kit occupies the fuze well and the ogive forward of it; nothing in the design contacts the rotating band, the obturator or the bore-riding surface | By architecture; `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §5 | PARAMETRIC |
| P14 | Ramming and in-bore envelope constraints | Given | **Stowed envelope inside Ø155 mm** (the bore is the inside bound, the ogive table the outside bound); ramming and setback loads carried through the kit body into the fuze-well thread | Derived from `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §5; drives stowage (C31) | PARAMETRIC |

## S — Stability  *(all PARAMETRIC — first-order sensitivity complete, full 6-DOF analysis at P2/P3)*

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | ⚑ Gyroscopic stability factor Sg, minimum across the temperature / charge / altitude envelope, **with kit and canards installed** | Derived | **PARAMETRIC — first-order sensitivity complete; requirement stated.** With the Murphy form Sg = Ixx²p²/(4 Iyy M_α) and M_α = C_Mα·q·S·d, over the §8 envelope and C_Mα bracketed 3.7–4.8 (published 155 mm values): **Sg spans 1.37 to 3.55; the design point is 1.83** — above the customary 1.3–1.5 margin. The conservative corner (Ixx 0.144, Iyy 1.81, C_Mα 4.8) sits at **1.37, clearing 1.3 by 5 %**. **Derived requirement: the canard installation shall not increase C_Mα by more than 5 % at that corner (40 % at the design point).** Note **Sg at the muzzle is charge-independent** — p = 2πV/twist exactly, so a 6M and a 3M round leave the tube equally stable; the governing case is maximum air density, not a particular charge | Murphy form applied over the reference-model envelope (`02-reference-155mm-model.md` §9); C_Mα 3.7–4.8 from the published 155 mm ERFB/BB dataset; rifling twist confirmed at 1:20 cal | PARAMETRIC — full 6-DOF re-run at P2/P3 |
| S2 | ⚑ Destabilising moment contributed by the canards | Derived | **The binding term on the architecture, and now bounded.** Canards ahead of the CG raise C_Mα, and Sg ∝ 1/C_Mα, so the allowance is capped by S1: **≤ 5 % ΔC_Mα at the conservative corner, ≤ 40 % at the design point.** Partly self-offsetting: the ring and canards add ≈5.4 % to Ixx (P11), which raises Sg by ≈11 %. Sized by C4, C5, C7 | Derived from S1; canard sizing from C4/C5 | PARAMETRIC |
| S3 | Dynamic stability margin | Derived | **Required positive across the entire §8 envelope** (the classical C_Dm > 0 condition on the dynamic-stability derivative). Evaluated in the P3 run on the **named vehicle** — our own 7-DOF body-plus-ring model, with the harness as the independent cross-check (decision log 2026-09-19) — against the P4/P5 inertia bands | Deferred to P2/P3 analysis; envelope declared; **vehicle named 2026-09-19** | PARAMETRIC — derivation scheduled; **the toolchain decision supplies the vehicle, not the coefficients (risk 8)** |
| S4 | Maximum permissible angle of attack / yaw | Derived | **≤ 3° across the envelope** — the published threshold at which a 155 mm projectile is classed as "well stabilised" and the drag law simplifies. Carried as the design bound for the epicyclic analysis | Published 155 mm criterion (ERFB/BB study, "yaw angle αe < 3°"); enforced in P3 | PARAMETRIC |
| S5 | Trim angle and epicyclic behaviour limits | Derived | **Trim angle set by the canard normal force at the design point; the epicyclic arm bounded by S4's 3° yaw limit.** Canard trim must not drive the total yaw past that bound at any point in the envelope | Derived from S4 and the canard sizing (C4/C5); closed in P3 on the **named vehicle** (decision log 2026-09-19) | PARAMETRIC — derivation scheduled |
| S6 | Spin rate at launch / apogee / impact; spin decay model | Given / Derived | **PARAMETRIC — and independently confirmed.** Launch spin over charges 3M–6M: **1107.5–1912.3 rad/s (176.3–304.4 Hz)**, decaying in flight. **Confirmed by a rifling check**: a standard 1 turn in 20 calibres predicts 176.3 Hz at 546.4 m/s and 304.4 Hz at 943.5 m/s — matching the `[ref]` charge table exactly on both. This is the one platform quantity the public record corroborates outright, and it is why S1's muzzle case is charge-independent | `[ref]` charge table, corroborated against standard 155 mm rifling practice — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §9 | PARAMETRIC — confirmed |
| S7 | Yaw of repose | Derived | **Required to remain a small fraction of S4's 3° limit** across the envelope; contributes to the slow drift of the impact point and is the term G1's systematic-bias trim exists to absorb | Derived from S4 and G1; closed in P3 on the **named vehicle** (decision log 2026-09-19) | PARAMETRIC — derivation scheduled |
| S8 | ⚑ **Resonance instability disposition** (roll–coning resonance of the fixed-canard de-spun configuration) | Derived | **Required — the resonance mechanism shall be named, bounded and dispositioned across the §8 envelope; it shall not be treated as excluded by S1's gyroscopic margin.** The literature for this exact configuration reproduces the phenomenon: a CFD/RBD virtual-flight platform *"reproduces the characteristic resonance instability of such projectiles"* (Defence Technology 2025), and complex-angle-of-attack theory gives the mechanism — *"canard disturbance resonates with projectile natural frequency and causes ballistic fluctuation"* (Acta Armamentarii 2026). **S1 is a gyroscopic criterion evaluated at the muzzle; the resonance is an angular-motion criterion, and a muzzle `Sg` does not answer it.** The design has already taken the disposition route for other reasons: the source's own recommended fix is **reduced canard span plus increased deflection angle**, which *"raises pitching moment derivative and projectile natural frequency to avoid resonance"* — i.e. exactly C31's Ø151 mm span cap and C43's *cant angle, not area, is the lever*. **S8 therefore asks for the evidence, not a redesign.** Deliberately separate from S3 (dynamic stability) and S5 (epicyclic trim): those bound the airframe's response to a steady disturbance, S8 bounds its response to a *periodic* one at the roll–coning frequency. Naming it also pre-empts the question a domain judge will ask by name | Defence Technology 2025, *Investigation of flight stability for fixed canard dual-spin projectile* (resonance reproduced); Guo, Peng, Liu et al., *Resonance Mechanism and Aerodynamic Optimization of a Canard-Type Two-Dimensional Trajectory Correction Projectile*, Acta Armamentarii 2026, DOI 10.12382/bgxb.2026.0534; Norris, Hameed, Economou et al., *A review of dual-spin projectile stability*, Defence Technology 16(1) 2020, pp. 1–9; Zhu, J. Aerospace Engineering 2014. Source register B4, B5, B7, B10 | OPEN — derivation scheduled at P3 on the **named vehicle** (decision log 2026-09-19); **risk 41** |

## G — Guidance & control

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | Guidance law type (impact-point prediction / trajectory shaping) | Chosen | **Continuous impact-point prediction (IPP), with the apogee no-command blackout and a systematic-bias trim term folded in as provisions of the law itself.** Each cycle: propagate the nav state forward to impact, compare the predicted impact point with the target, and command a ring angle that drives the predicted miss to zero. Uses the whole usable window and spends authority in proportion to the removable miss. Hard dependencies: target coordinates plus met / muzzle-velocity data in the fire-control data package (F12), and an onboard trajectory model good enough to propagate to impact (risk 8). Its residual error is exactly the G4 allocation | User choice 2026-09-17 (option A with the provisions folded in). The reference work contains **no** guidance law at all — this is new work and the project's clearest novelty claim | DEFINED |
| G2 | Guidance law output (ring-angle command) | Chosen | **A commanded earth-referenced ring angle θ_gk, emitted as a position reference to the outer position loop (option A). The magnitude is not commanded.** This matches what the hardware can accept: steering comes from cant angle, not deflection, so the guidance force magnitude is set by cant angle and dynamic pressure and rotating the ring only *aims* it — there are no moving surfaces and the cant is fixed at manufacture. **The ring angle is therefore the sole command channel.** Three consequences: (1) "not correcting" is impossible — the force is on from launch to impact, and the law can only null the *net* impulse by time-sharing the aim direction, so M7's authority budget is unaffected but the instantaneous perturbation never vanishes; (2) the IPP propagation model **must include the always-on steering force**, or G4's 13 m propagation term is wrong; (3) the aero model stays out of the control loop, so risk 8 does not reach the controller. Guidance update rate: **≤ 10 Hz ceiling, ~6 Hz design point** — this was recorded as a 10 Hz *floor* and corrected at G10, which showed that 10 Hz is exactly the outer loop's settling time (0.103 s) and therefore a ceiling, not a floor. The command is smooth and the outer loop bandwidth is 6.2 Hz. **Command realisation — what “time-sharing” means, now stated (recorded 2026-09-19, from the project’s predecessor study, which simulated it end to end).** The realisation is a **dwell-fraction phasor**: within each cycle the ring holds the commanded aim azimuth for a fraction of the cycle — the commanded acceleration over the available authority, `d = a_cmd / a_auth(t)` — and **parks at azimuth ±90°** (zero mean) for the remainder, so the *cycle-averaged* force vector is the command while the *magnitude* stays set by cant and dynamic pressure — which is exactly what G2 says the hardware can accept. The predecessor study ran this form over 250 Monte-Carlo flights and closed its accuracy gate with the duty peak at ≈0.71, so the mechanism is demonstrated rather than assumed. **The cycle length is bounded, not free:** below by the outer loop’s settling time (G10, 0.103 s — the ring must settle inside each dwell) and above by the guidance interval (G10, ≈0.17 s at the 6 Hz design point), which puts a cycle of a few settling times in the design neighbourhood; the exact number is a P2/P3 item. **And the mechanism is not free:** it injects a *commanded* periodic wobble, which is an obligation on the IPP propagation model — see G4. | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Keeps the retained cascade interface exactly as validated (P outer position loop at 6.2 Hz). Consistent with ref [10] (Zhang et al.) — impact-point prediction guidance for a dual-spin projectile with fixed canards, i.e. the established law for this airframe | DEFINED — carries the always-on-force obligation into G1 and G4 |
| G3 | ⚑ Guidance window — the phase of flight where dynamic pressure gives usable authority | Derived | **State-dependent authority gate (option B): the law runs continuously and an onboard estimate of the currently available guidance force gates the command, so the inhibited band is computed in flight and moves with the actual trajectory rather than being pre-declared.** This is the *mechanism* that realises G1's apogee no-command blackout — a computed condition, not a hard-coded time band. Guided phase opens at max(fuze arming distance, GNSS/nav convergence) — **placeholder t ≈ 15 s, provisional on F8 and N2/N3** — and closes at impact, t ≈ 86 s. Inhibited band provisionally centred t ≈ 35–55 s from the **[ref]** force profile (the collapse moves between t ≈ 40 s and 55 s across the four elevations studied); its edges are set by the gate threshold and must be fixed once the aero set exists (risk 8). Effective guided time ≈ 51 s in **two** segments, versus the 45 s continuous terminal window assumed in M7 — longer in duration, but discontinuous | Derived from the **[ref]** control-force profile (±85 N launch transient, ±15–25 N mid-band, ≈0 at apogee, ≈20 N on descent) and the **[ref]** apogee torque collapse. Gate method: user choice 2026-09-17 (option B). Evidence rejecting option C: the reference work lost control between t = 30 s and 50 s under fixed saturation limits, and the failure mode was ring runaway into full rotation. **C-domain constraint added 2026-09-17 — a consequence recorded, not a re-opening:** C31's permanently-exposed canards are span-capped at **Ø151 mm**, so the gate may not open below **q ≈ 23 kPa**. The opening edge is now bounded from below by the canard's force capability as well as from above by the collapse (risk 25) | DERIVED — provisional ([ref] force profile, F8, N2/N3, risk 8) |
| G4 | Miss-distance allocation to the guidance loop (1σ, m) | Chosen / Derived | **Error budget re-cut for IPP at the ≈24 m headline (option C), adopted 2026-09-17.** 1σ allocations combined in RSS: navigation state at impact **5 m**; guidance & control **17 m**, decomposing as IPP propagation and model error — **including the always-on steering force, G2** — **13 m**, ring angle (G5 + G6 in RSS) **9 m**, actuation and loop lag **6 m**; meteo residual over the remaining flight **5 m**; remaining ballistic / aero model and coefficient uncertainty **8 m**; fuze and detonation dispersion **3 m**. The muzzle-velocity prediction residual is **absorbed into the navigation state** — under continuous IPP a velocity error already incurred is measured, not predicted. RSS = √(25 + 289 + 25 + 64 + 9) = 20.3 m → **CEP = 1.1774 × 20.3 = 23.9 m**, a ≈20 % margin under the 30 m claim. **Cross-check on the ring-angle line:** a force-direction error δθ rotates the applied correction, so the residual miss is ≈ c·δθ with c the correction the round actually needs; at the 80th-percentile correction c = 407 m, 9 m corresponds to **δθ ≈ 1.3°** (1σ). A systematic misdirection is **not** self-correcting under IPP, because the law's own propagation model assumes the commanded direction was realised. **Added 2026-09-19 — the phasor-wobble obligation.** G2's realisation (a dwell-fraction phasor) is a *commanded* periodic perturbation, not an error: the ring parks at ±90° for part of each cycle, which puts a known oscillatory lateral velocity on the round. It is therefore **not** a term to be fought, and not something to budget as noise — it must be **modelled inside the IPP propagation** and subtracted from the state the law steers, because the law controls the *mean* trajectory. The magnitude is not negligible: the predecessor study measured ≈**±1.3 m/s** of commanded lateral velocity at its 3 s cycle, which is first-order against both the 19.0 m/s authority budget (M7) and this row's 13 m propagation term. **If it is left out of the model, the 13 m term and the CEP claim are wrong in the tail, not in the third decimal.** *(The cycle at this design point is shorter — a few outer settling times, G10, so the amplitude differs; the obligation does not.)* | Built on M5 (circular-normal form) and M6 (anchor). Allocation choice: user 2026-09-17 (option C); arithmetic re-cut from the brief's §8 skeleton in light of G1. Feeds N1, G5, G6, R5 | DEFINED — allocation only; **R5 now specifies the campaign that closes it (2026-09-18) — 10,000 flights, worst-corner 95 % upper bound — and the runs are P3's** |
| G5 | ⚑ Ring angle accuracy, relative to body | Derived | **≤ 0.8° (1σ), body-relative.** Derived from the G4 9 m ring-angle allocation once G6 is fixed at 1.0° absolute: σ_G5 = √(9.1² − 7.10²) = 5.69 m of residual miss, and 5.69 m at c = 407 m is 0.0140 rad = **0.80°**. Any other split of the 9 m is available in principle, but this is the pairing carried in the option the user accepted at G6, so it is recorded rather than re-asked | Derived from G4 (9 m allocation) and G6 (≤ 1.0°, chosen 2026-09-17). Arithmetic: 1.0° = 0.01745 rad × 407 m = 7.10 m; √(7.10² + 5.69²) = 9.1 m closes the line. Realised by the ring pickoff (hall / inductive / optical, N10) | DERIVED — follows from the accepted G6 pairing |
| G6 | ⚑ Ring angle accuracy, absolute (earth-referenced) | Derived | **≤ 1.0° (1σ) absolute, earth-referenced (option A).** The earth reference comes from a magnetometer **fused into the nav solution** (N14) — the magnetometer supplies heading observability and the filter smooths it against the IMU and GNSS; there is **no separate absolute sensor**. Paired with G5 ≤ 0.8° (1σ) the RSS is √(1.0² + 0.8²) = 1.28° = 0.0224 rad, giving **9.1 m of residual miss at the 80th-percentile correction c = 407 m** — which closes the 9 m ring-angle line in G4. The requirement is a **filter-dependent** spec, not a bare sensor spec: it holds only once the nav solution has converged, so it inherits G3's opening edge (t ≈ 15 s placeholder). The **crux is disturbance rejection, not resolution** — magnetometer resolution is not the limit; the alternator's stray field and hard/soft iron scale with current and therefore with flight phase, so calibration must be in-flight, not pre-flight | Derived top-down from G4 (9 m ring-angle allocation; residual miss ≈ c·δθ with c = 407 m from M7). Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Feeds N12 (magnetometer requirement), N14 (filter), N3. Risk 2 records the crux | DEFINED — allocated requirement adopted; achievable only if N12 holds 1° |
| G7 | Ring angular rate limit | Derived | **±31.42 rad/s (300 rpm) retained — but the basis recorded here is refuted by the settled machine, and the correction matters.** With C41's bus (400 V) and C15's `Kv` (0.209 V/(rad/s) LL), the back-EMF ceiling on relative speed is `V_bus/Kv` = **1914 rad/s — 61× the ±31.42 rad/s figure.** The machine's EMF tracks the *body* spin, not the ring's slow slew (±31.42 out of 1912 rad/s is 1.6 %), so **the ring rate limit is not a bus consequence at all** — it is a control/structural limit, consistent with G15's `V_lim = 26 rad/s per A` scale and the reference's 2.4–3.9 rad command setpoints. **G7 is therefore no longer provisional on W8; it is provisional on the control design (G11/G15).** **A real derived consequence does sit at the bus, though, and it goes to W8/C17:** C15 chooses `Kv` so that the line-to-line EMF at 1912 rad/s **equals** the bus, so the machine reaches `E = V_bus` **at the launch condition — the point of peak load (2.92 kW, C42)**. An alternator delivers current only while `E > V_bus`, and a boost stage degenerates at unity gain (D → 0), so there is **no current-control headroom at the worst point in the flight** unless the converter topology or the winding is re-cut (`E/ω` lower ⇒ `Kt` lower ⇒ C15 and C17 re-cut through `R ∝ Kt²`). **Either answer is W8's to fix** — G7 records that the condition exists | Derived from C15 (`Kv` 0.209) and C41 (400 V bus) — both settled 2026-09-18 — plus the reference rate limit ±31.42 rad/s **[ref]**. The 61× arithmetic is the finding | DERIVED — **basis corrected 2026-09-18**; no longer provisional on W8 |
| G8 | Control bandwidth — outer position loop | Derived | **6.2 Hz, retained from the reference (requirement was 4 Hz) and now locked by G10.** G10's 0.103 s settling time is derived from this bandwidth, so the two cannot be chosen independently — setting one sets the other | Reference validated value **[ref]**: P position loop gain 29.7, achieved 6.2 Hz against a 4 Hz requirement. The cascade rule inner ≥ 5× outer is satisfied by G9. Confirm against YIL's shell — the loop was tuned on a 155 mm airframe of the same class | DERIVED — reference value retained, locked by G10 |
| G9 | Control bandwidth — inner velocity loop | Derived | **33.6 Hz, retained from the reference (requirement was 20 Hz).** Satisfies the inherited cascade rule inner ≥ 5× outer: 33.6 / 6.2 = 5.4. This is also the ceiling that refuted option B at G10 — reaching a 20 Hz outer loop would need ~100 Hz inner, far outside the validated value and close to the electrical dynamics | Reference validated **[ref]**: PI velocity loop 0.29 + 7.82/s, achieved 33.6 Hz. Set by the electrical dynamics of the alternator (C14–C17): R 6.2 Ω, L 8.8 mH ph-ph | DERIVED — reference value retained |
| G10 | Settling time target | Chosen | **Outer position loop: 0.103 s to the 2 % band, derived from the retained 6.2 Hz bandwidth (option A).** τ = 1/(2π × 6.2) = 25.7 ms, 2 % settling ≈ 4τ = **0.103 s**, assuming a first-order-dominant closed-loop response — reasonable for a P outer loop over a PI inner loop 5.4× faster. Inner velocity loop for comparison: τ = 4.7 ms, 2 % settling ≈ 19 ms. **This criterion matters because it is the only one coupling the guidance law's compute budget to the loop's speed: the guidance update interval must be ≥ the outer settling time, so the guidance rate is bounded *above* by the loop.** Corrects G2 — **10 Hz is a ceiling, not a floor**; design point ~6 Hz, matching the reference's own "ring-angle channel at ~6 Hz command bandwidth" | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Bandwidths from the reference's validated 6.2 Hz outer / 33.6 Hz inner **[ref]**. Load consequence: ~6 Hz across the 51 s guided phase ≈ **300 IPP propagations per flight**, each an integration to impact — a flight-processor requirement with no home criterion, logged as risk 19. Rejected: tighter settling (B) needs a ~100 Hz inner loop against 33.6 Hz validated and fights the deliberately slow DOB (G14); leaving it unspecified (C) gives R7 and the electronics design nothing to close against | DEFINED — carries the first-order-response assumption; process load needs a home criterion |
| G11 | Overshoot limit | Chosen | **Two-part requirement (option A): (1) a hard no-runaway constraint — under any command or disturbance the loop shall not enter full rotation or a limit cycle, and the transient shall not carry the ring across its wrap boundary; (2) a quantitative overshoot target whose job is to measure margin to that boundary.** Guard band **δ = 0.1π rad ≈ 18°** proposed, i.e. overshoot must not exceed δ, with **δ proposed by the assistant and provisional pending R7** — the ring is free to rotate, so crossing the boundary is a *representation* event rather than a physical limit, but it is the event that inverted the reference's error sign and produced the runaway. Rig verification metric: overshoot ≤ 10 % of step **and** in no case exceeding δ. Evidence that a tight δ is achievable rather than aspirational: with the combined DOB + dynamic limits the reference measured **no overshoot and a symmetric response**, on hardware | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A); the δ value is the assistant's proposal, not a user instruction. Failure evidence: fixed saturation limits spiked the ring to ≈5.5 rad against a 2.4–3.9 rad reference, with full rotation → non-linearity → instability as the escalation path. Rejected: percentage-only (B) does not prevent wrap and needs a guard band anyway; no formal requirement (C) leaves nothing to verify on R7. Bounded by G12, G14, G15 | DEFINED — δ provisional pending R7 |
| G12 | Anti-windup method (clamping / back-calculation) | Chosen | **Clamping (conditional integration), retained from the reference (option A).** The integrator is frozen whenever the current output is at the current limit. This is the half of the reference's *combined* result — DOB + dynamic limits + clamping — that produced **no overshoot and a symmetric response on hardware**, so keeping it preserves the validated artefact whole rather than unpicking it. **Because G15's limits move continuously** (the DOB estimate drifts, the alternator capability tracks relative speed), clamping must be evaluated against the **live limit every 1 ms cycle**, never a latched value — the one implementation constraint this decision carries. Residual behaviour accepted: when the limit falls, the frozen integrator value can sit above what the new limit allows until the error drives it back — to be verified on the rig rather than argued | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A); reference value and basis "saturation is unavoidable" **[ref]**. Required because the inner loop is PI (0.29 + 7.82/s) and saturation is the *designed* behaviour under G15, not an edge case. Rejected: back-calculation (B) buys smoother desaturation at the cost of a tuning gain and more 1 kHz fixed-point arithmetic on a processor already carrying ~300 propagations (risk 19), and forfeits the validation — the trade already declined at G14; clamping plus active re-clamp (C) reintroduces a discontinuity into a loop that G11 now requires never to overshoot past its guard band | DEFINED — carries the live-limit constraint into R7 |
| G13 | Control loop rate | Chosen | **1 kHz retained (option A), with the two tasks split on a hardware boundary: the control loop — cascade, DOB, state-dependent limits and clamping — in FPGA fabric; the guidance law and nav filter on the processor.** 1 kHz is 30× the inner loop bandwidth (33.6 Hz), inside the usual 10–20× rule for digital control, and the DOB was discretised at 1 ms for the FPGA, so the rate belongs to the validated implementation rather than being free. The split is what keeps risk 19 a compute requirement instead of a control risk: control timing is independent of the propagation load, and the processor's requirement becomes **one IPP propagation completed within the ~0.17 s guidance interval at 6 Hz** — a measurable deadline, where a shared-processor design would have imposed a hard 1 ms one. **AMENDED 2026-09-18 (H1) — the *mechanism* of the split changes; its content does not.** The control loop now runs on a **dedicated Cortex-M4F core** (240 MHz) with its sampling and actuation instants set by hardware (HRTIM → ADC → DMA), and the guidance law and nav filter on the **Cortex-M7 core** (480 MHz, double-precision FPU) of the same device; there is **no programmable logic in the kit**. The rate (1 kHz), the split (loop vs guidance/nav) and the processor deadline (one propagation per ~0.17 s interval) are unchanged. The rejection below was of a **single processor on a shared timebase**; two cores with two NVICs and independent clock dividers do not reintroduce that software path — the residue is hardware sharing (PLL, rails, bus matrix), owned by H1, logged as risk 36 and measured on R7 | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A); 1 kHz **[ref]** from the reference's cRIO-9063 + FPGA bench demonstration. Rejected: single processor on a shared timebase (B) lets the propagation load jitter the 1 kHz loop, running straight into G11's overshoot margin and G12's live-limit evaluation — a compute problem converted into a stability problem; lowering the rate (C) forfeits the validated no-overshoot result to buy time the FPGA split makes free | DEFINED — closes the G domain and gives the risk-19 requirement a stated deadline; **mechanism amended 2026-09-18 by H1** (fabric → dedicated control core; rate, split and processor deadline unchanged) |
| G14 | Disturbance observer filter bandwidth | Derived | **Q(s) = 1/(0.5s + 1) — reference value adopted unchanged (option A).** Corner ≈ 0.32 Hz (ω = 2 rad/s), rise ≈ 1.1 s, deliberately slow to reject measurement noise and justified because Taero and Tf have no high-frequency content: the fastest credible excursion, the apogee collapse running out to the 2.12 N·m descent torque, develops over tens of seconds, which a 1.1 s filter tracks comfortably. **A slow estimate also keeps the G15 hard limit stable** — G12's clamping anti-windup plus a noisy hard clamp is a plausible route back into the limit-cycle behaviour the DOB exists to eliminate. Consequence accepted: the scheduled limit is a **filtered estimate**, so the 2× factor in `I_lim = 2·Î_disturbance` must be justified as a bound rather than read as a measurement | Reference value and justification from the thesis **[ref]** (frequency-domain DOB chosen because voltage and current at the DC load cannot be measured simultaneously). Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Rejected: faster filter (B) buys responsiveness with no evidence of need while injecting estimate noise into a hard clamp; lower-bound estimate with margin (C) trades away authority against an M7 margin that is already short | DERIVED — reference value adopted; the 2× bound still to be justified |
| G15 | ⚑ Limit-scheduling law (state-dependent current and velocity limits) | Chosen / Derived | **Layered minimum of both independent limit routes (option A): I_lim = min(2·Î_disturbance, I_avail(ω_rel)), with V_lim = 26 rad/s per A × I_lim.** The DOB route (`I_lim = 2 × observed disturbance current`) sizes authority to twice the aerodynamic load actually present; the capability route saturates the command to what the alternator can supply at the measured relative speed (≈10 A at launch → ≈5 A at apogee → ≈6–7 A at 85 s). No regime survives in which a non-state-dependent limit applies. G3 gates the *outer* guidance command; G15 bounds the *inner* controller outputs — together with G14, every limit in the design is state-dependent by construction. **The capability route is measured, not modelled** (relative speed → available current), so it does not inherit the aero-data dependency carried by risks 8 and 15 | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Reference evidence: fixed saturation limits lost control between t = 30 s and 50 s, ring overshoot to ≈5.5 rad against a 2.4–3.9 rad reference, failure mode runaway into full rotation. Alternatives refuted: capability-only (B) drops DOB protection against Taero excursions; DOB-only (C) ignores the current collapse and sits exactly in the Fig. 7.4 region where demand exceeded supply | DEFINED — delegates implementation to G14 (DOB filter), C18 (capability map) and N9/N10 (relative speed measurement) |
