## C — Canard actuation assembly

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Canard count and arrangement (steering pair + control pair) | Chosen | **Four fixed-incidence canards on the de-spun ring, in two pairs: steering pair at 0°/180° (equal cant → lateral components add, roll torques cancel → net guidance force) and control pair at 90°/−90° (opposite cant → lateral components cancel, tangential add → zero net force, net roll torque).** No moving control surfaces; the ring roll angle θ_gk is the sole command channel (G2). **The control pair is load-bearing, not optional:** the alternator is a generator whose reaction torque acts in one sense only, so the control pair's aerodynamic torque is the *only* source of ring torque in the opposite sense — it is what makes `Taero > Tf` (C10) satisfiable at all. Sizing forms: **F_steer = 2·F·cos δ_s** (feeds C8), **T_aero = 2·F·sin δ_c·r** (feeds C9, C11, C12). Canard count corroborated by the fielded M1156 PGK — four fixed canards | User choice 2026-09-17 (option A). Reference arrangement **[ref]**; count corroborated against the fielded M1156 PGK public specification (`02-reference-155mm-model.md` §7) | DEFINED |
| C2 | Cant angle — steering pair | Chosen | | Sets F_steer | OPEN |
| C3 | Cant angle — control pair | Chosen | | Sets Taero | OPEN |
| C4 | ⚑ Canard planform and reference area | Derived | | From C8 and G3 | OPEN |
| C5 | Canard span, root and tip chord | Derived | | Must stow (C31) | OPEN |
| C6 | Canard section / aerofoil | Chosen | | Mach 0.5–2.5 operation | OPEN |
| C7 | Ring radius / canard moment arm (r, R) | Chosen | | Sets torque per unit force | OPEN |
| C8 | ⚑ Net guidance force required | Derived | **F_steer ≥ 18.1 N at the design point — and it is a *minimum over the guided window*, not an average.** Derived from M7's authority requirement: **F = 2·A·m/T²** with A = 407 m, m = 45.0 kg, T = 45 s → **18.1 N**; on G3's full 51 s two-segment guided phase the requirement relaxes to **14.1 N**. The figure is a **lower bound**: it assumes full force across the whole window, so G3's gated window makes the true requirement higher by an amount that stays unquantified until R5 (risk 10). **The requirement is on the minimum because the force is not commandable (G2)** — F = q·S_c·C_Nδ·δ_s tracks dynamic pressure, which is exactly why G3's gate exists — so the sizing case is the worst point still inside the guided phase (C12). **Critical consequence: 18.1 N sits above the floor of the reference's ±15–25 N band, and 15 N is not sufficient** — F = 15 N gives A = 337.5 m and captures only **67 %** of the unguided population, the exact failure M7's decision log rejected. **The design cannot inherit the reference canard sizing.** Transient case carried to C28: **±85 N at launch**. Steering-pair form: F_steer = 2·F·cos δ_s (C1) | Derived from M7 (A = 407 m) and the reference-model design mass (45.0 kg) — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §8; window from G3. **Supersedes the `[ref]` ±15–25 N band as a requirement** — the band is now evidence of what the reference achieved, not the criterion | DERIVED — provisional (M7 anchor + G3 window) |
| C9 | ⚑ Torque authority — symmetric criterion | Derived | **`Taero ≤ Talt_max(ω_rel)/2 + Tf`, evaluated against the *instantaneous* available current at every flight condition — not a single number.** Derivation: the alternator is a generator whose reaction torque acts in one sense only, so `+X authority = Talt_max + Tf − Taero` and `−X authority = Taero − Tf`; setting them equal gives the symmetric form. **Corrects the inherited equation** — the reference's published `(Talt_max + Tf)/2` drops one friction term and is symmetric only to within 3.5 %: at `Talt_max = Kt × 8 A = 5.728 N·m` it gives **2.91 N·m** against the exact **2.96 N·m**. **The criterion is state-dependent** because `Talt_max = Kt·I_avail(ω_rel)` and available current falls with relative speed: **launch ≈10 A → 3.68 N·m; 8 A design point → 2.96 N·m; apogee ≈5 A → 1.89 N·m**. **The binding case is descent, not apogee** — with `Taero = 2.119 N·m` at t = 86.5 s the criterion demands `I ≥ 2(Taero − Tf)/Kt = 5.64 A` against ≈5 A available: an **11 % shortfall in transient authority at the end of flight**, where holding authority is unaffected (needs only 2.82 A). Closing routes: alternator low-relative-speed capability (C18/C19) or control-pair cant (C3). Applies the G15 principle — every limit state-dependent by construction | Derived from C1 (the control pair is the only −X torque source), C14 (Kt = 0.716 N·m/A), C18 (I_avail ≈10 → 5 A **[ref]**), Tf = 0.1 N·m **[ref]**, and the flight-replay `Taero` profile **[ref]**. Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A) | DERIVED — provisional (C18 current profile) |
| C10 | ⚑ Torque authority — minimum controllability: Taero > Tf | Derived | | Sizing equation | OPEN |
| C11 | Taero maximum over the envelope | Derived | ≈ 2.2 N·m **[ref]** | 6-DOF + aero. **Vehicle named 2026-09-19 (decision log); stays OPEN — the toolchain supplies the vehicle and not the coefficients (risk 8), and no value may close on a harness-only result (risk 38)** | OPEN |
| C12 | ⚑ Taero minimum (near apogee) | Derived | **`Taero_min` = 0.1706 N·m at t = 41.16 s.** It tracks dynamic pressure (`Taero = ½ρV²·S·l_gk·C_rc`), so its minimum follows the q minimum near apogee, against the measured `Tf` = 0.1 N·m: a **1.7:1 margin**, the thinnest in the design. **The derived result is the box it closes with C9.** Writing the control pair's size as a scale factor k on the reference `Taero` curve: C10 at apogee gives `k > Tf/0.1706`; C9 at descent — the binding end, with `Talt_max = 0.716 × 5 A = 3.58 N·m` — gives `k ≤ (1.79 + Tf)/2.119`. **The bounds cross at `Tf* = 0.1567 N·m`**: above that, no control-pair size satisfies both criteria. At the measured 0.1 N·m the window is **k ∈ (0.586, 0.892)** — the control pair must be sized within ≈±20 % of the reference. The geometric mid k = 0.723 gives `Taero_min` = 0.123 N·m (1.23:1 over friction) and `Taero_max` = 1.53 N·m, inside C9's 1.89 N·m limit. **The friction budget therefore decides whether a control pair exists at all** — a derived hard constraint feeding C13. The constraining shape parameter is the ratio **`Taero_max/Taero_min` = 12.4**, which survives the aero re-baseline as a ratio even when both endpoints move | Derived from the reference flight-replay `Taero` profile **[ref]**, C9, C10 and C14. Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A) | DERIVED — provisional (reference aero; ratio 12.4 is the transferable quantity) |
| C13 | ⚑ Friction budget Tf (maximum allowable, all speeds and temperatures) | Derived | **`Tf ≤ 0.10 N·m, flat across the entire envelope** — all ring-to-body relative speeds to ≈1900 rad/s (the launch-spin extreme) and the full operating temperature range — **inside C12's feasibility gate of 0.1567 N·m. The flatness requirement is the actual specification:** the bearing selection shall be load-dominated, not viscous-dominated. **The inherited datum does not survive scrutiny.** `Tf = 0.1 N·m` was measured at **800 rpm relative = 83.8 rad/s**, and that is a *rig* speed: the rig scaled body spin to 83.7 rad/s for a flight 1623 rad/s — a **19.4× reduction**. The ring is de-spun, so in flight the relative speed ≈ body spin (**1352 rad/s at apogee, 1623 at launch**). The reference's own viscous coefficient **`b = 1.273e-3 N·m·s/rad`** reproduces the measured point exactly (0.107 N·m at 83.8 rad/s ✓) and then **fails C10 by an order of magnitude in flight**: **1.72 N·m at apogee against `Taero_min` = 0.17 N·m**, and **2.07 N·m at launch against `Taero_max` ≈ 2.1 N·m**. **So `b` is a rig-loss parameter** — slip ring, coupling, seals, load bank, all absent in flight — **not a flight friction law, and must not be carried into flight.** Two obligations follow: the bearing selection shall demonstrate a speed-flat friction characteristic (C22), and **R7's rig shall characterise ring friction at flight-representative relative speed**. **Thermal consequence (added 2026-09-19): this budget is also a *heat* budget.** `Tf × ω_rel` at 0.1 N·m against the ring-to-body relative speed (≈1400–1900 rad/s) is **≈140–190 W continuous, ≈12–17 kJ per flight** — roughly a tenth of C42's 121 kJ — and it is dissipated **in the bearings rather than in the dump** (W7). Two consequences follow: the friction budget is the one C-domain number that appears in the thermal ledger, and preload (C23) therefore buys stiffness (C35) and spends friction *and* heat — the C23 trade is a three-way one, not a two-way one | Derived from C10/C12 (the feasibility gate) and the reference's measured friction plus viscous model **[ref]**. Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). **Trade owner assigned 2026-09-18: C23** — the preload that holds C35's `k ≥ 122 N/µm` is the same knob that spends this budget, so both are set together at C23 | DERIVED — provisional (C25 must confirm the speed dependence by test) |
| C14 | Alternator torque constant Kt | Chosen | **0.362 N·m/A (rms line current) — sizing result, 2026-09-18, confirmed by the team.** Convention **adopted**: the register quotes `Kt` in N·m per ampere **rms line current** and `Kv` in V **rms line-to-line** per rad/s, so `Kt = √3·Kv` holds by construction. **The `[ref]` 0.716 / 0.749 pair is relabelled as a different convention** (it satisfies the `Kt ≈ Kv` SI identity instead) **and must never be quoted alongside these values** — see `03-caa-machine-sizing.md` §6. Internal check: 0.362 × 15.9 A = **5.76 N·m** = C19 | Sizing §6; supersedes the `[ref]` value | DEFINED 2026-09-18 |
| C15 | Alternator voltage constant Kv | Chosen | **0.209 V/(rad/s) line-to-line — sizing result, 2026-09-18, confirmed by the team**, settled against what was then a 400 V bus at 1912 rad/s. **`Kv` is a machine constant and is unaffected by the bus revision** — H8/H12 moved the bus to ≈150–200 V (180 V nominal) and deliberately left the winding alone (C17), so this value stands, and E_min = 0.209 × 1107.5 ≈ 231 V is what the revised bus is set *below*. The bus is a *free* choice at constant copper loss (`R ∝ Kt²` at fixed slot area), so it was settled on partial-discharge grounds at altitude rather than on efficiency. The reference's 0.749 implies a 1.4 kV machine and a 1.4 kV rectifier. | Sizing §6; supersedes the `[ref]` value; **bus revision from H8/H12** | DEFINED 2026-09-18 — bus and winding settled as a pair, **bus since revised (C41, H8, H12); `Kv` unchanged** |
| C16 | Alternator pole pairs | Chosen | **7 retained — 14 poles / 12 slots — and confirmed by sizing rather than inherited.** The yoke thickness is set by the pole pitch, so pole count trades slot room against iron loss; p = 7 is the balance point, giving a 4.74 mm yoke with a 19.6 mm slot at 2 130 Hz. **Iron loss at that frequency remains the one untrustworthy number and is C39's to close.** | Sizing §3 | DEFINED 2026-09-18 |
| C17 | Alternator phase resistance and inductance | Chosen | Set by the turns count, which is **free at constant copper loss** (`R ∝ Kt²` at fixed slot area). To be fixed with the winding design once C41's bus is confirmed. **Cross-ref (2026-09-19): the winding is the variable W8 acts on** — if W8 takes the EMF-headroom route, the turns count, `Kt` and `R` all move together here, and C19's 5.76 N·m at 15.9 A rms must still hold after the re-cut. **Resolved 2026-09-19 (H8): the winding route was *not* taken — the turns count, `Kt` and `R` stand as sized (C14/C19 unchanged) and the bus moves instead; C17's R and L remain winding-design outputs against the lowered bus.** | Sizing §2, §6 | OPEN |
| C18 | Maximum alternator current (and its variation with relative speed) | Derived | **15.9 A rms at peak torque; 6.5 A rms at the holding duty** — **torque-set by `Kt`, so unchanged by the bus revision**: the 2026-09-18 sizing pass quoted these against a 400 V bus, and **H8/H12 (2026-09-19) set the bus at ≈150–200 V, 180 V nominal**, without moving them. Corresponding current densities: **7.5 A/mm² peak (transient slew), 3 A/mm² continuous (holding)** — both well inside limits. The speed dependence of the *available* current still governs C9. *(Bus provenance: `03-caa-machine-sizing.md` §4's 400 V / 1 400 V table is a historical trade record — see the superseded notice at the head of that file.)* | Sizing §4, §6; bus revision from H8/H12 | DERIVED — confirmed by the team 2026-09-18 |
| C19 | Maximum alternator torque Talt_max | Derived | **5.76 N·m retained, and confirmed achievable**: 7.5 A/mm² peak is a transient duty the winding carries without thermal penalty, and the continuous holding duty sits at only 3 A/mm². Closed on the register's own constants: `Kt` 0.362 × 15.9 A = 5.76 N·m (C14 × C18). | Sizing §4; C9 × C18 | DERIVED — confirmed by the team 2026-09-18 |
| C20 | Alternator continuous power rating | Chosen | **REVISED: ≈3.0 kW, not 1.2 kW [ref].** The machine is a *loaded generator*, not an idler: `P = Taero × ω_body` peaks at **2.92 kW** and integrates to **≈121 kJ** over the flight. The reference's 1.2 kW rating is **exceeded ×2.4** through most of the trajectory — see C42. **This is a rating, not a dissipation specification:** the 121 kJ is what W7 must shed, and the machine's own thermal path is only one of the places it can go. | Sizing §7 | DERIVED — supersedes the `[ref]` value; **confirmed by the team 2026-09-18** |
| C21 | Ring inertia Jgk | Derived | 1.197e-3 kg·m² **[ref]** | CAD mass properties | OPEN |
| C22 | Bearing type and selection | Chosen | | From C13, C24, C26 | OPEN |
| C23 | Bearing preload | Chosen | **OWNER ASSIGNED 2026-09-18 of the C35 ↔ C13 preload tension** — preload is the single knob that buys C35's radial pair stiffness (`k ≥ 122 N/µm`) and costs C13's friction (`Tf ≤ 0.10 N·m`, flat over the speed envelope), so the two are resolved **here**, not in their own rows. **The value shall be the minimum preload that holds `k_pair ≥ 122 N/µm` at launch while keeping `Tf ≤ 0.10 N·m` across the envelope, on C22's load-dominated bearing.** Both constraints are provisional — C35 on the CAD load path, C13 on C25's test — so the value is fixed with C22 and re-checked at P2 | Feeds C13 (friction) and C35 (stiffness); verified by C25 by test and on the R7 rig | OPEN — **trade owner; value to be set with C22** |
| C24 | Bearing life (revolutions / operating duration) | Derived | | ≈90 s flight + storage | OPEN |
| C25 | Bearing friction torque versus relative speed | Derived | | Must satisfy C13 | OPEN |
| C26 | ⚑ Ring setback load (governing structural case for the CAD) | Derived | **73.5 kN ≈ 74 kN peak axial setback** (0.5 kg ring assembly × 15 000 g), with **24.5 kN radial** (× 5 000 g) alongside — a **3:1 axial-dominant combined load** carried for the setback pulse. Scales linearly with ring mass: 0.3 kg → 44.1 kN, 0.4 → 58.8, 0.5 → 73.5, 0.6 → 88.2 kN, so **every 0.1 kg of ring costs 14.7 kN of retention**. **The structural conclusion: 74 kN peak cannot pass through the flight bearings.** The thin-section instrument bearings this assembly needs (≈30–50 mm bore) carry static load ratings in the single-digit kilonewtons — the peak is well over an order of magnitude above that, and buying a bearing that carries it would be larger, heavier and higher-friction, breaking the ≤ 1.4 kg kit budget (P9) and reopening C13 and risk 22. **So the requirement is not "a ring that survives 74 kN" but "a launch load path that bypasses the flight bearings":** C32 must provide a setback-actuated lock, or a seat the ring bears against under acceleration and releases from as the load decays. **Stated gap:** E1 gives a bare g-level — the **pulse shape and duration** are needed, for the retention structure (which needs the impulse) as distinct from the bearing criterion (which needs the peak, for brinelling) | Derived from E1 (15 000 g axial **[ref]**), E2 (5 000 g radial **[ref]**) and the 0.5 kg ring assumption inside the P9 kit budget. Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A) | DERIVED — provisional (ring mass pending C21; E1 pulse shape pending) |
| C27 | Canard steady aerodynamic load | Derived | | From C8 | OPEN |
| C28 | Canard launch transient load | Derived | ≈85 N **[ref]** | Structural design case | OPEN |
| C29 | Deployment release mechanism and energy source (setback / spin / spring) | Chosen | **Not applicable — closed by C31 (user decision 2026-09-17).** The canards are permanently exposed, so there is no release mechanism and no stored deployment energy. **This removes a stored-energy hazard from the kit for its entire shelf life**, and with it the drop/shock (E4) and storage-life (F14) obligations that a cocked spring would have carried. It also removes the deployment transient against q_max = 545 kPa at muzzle exit, which was the sizing case for a spring | C31 user decision 2026-09-17 | DEFINED — closed by architecture |
| C30 | Deployment lock design and retention force | Chosen | **Not applicable — closed by C31 (user decision 2026-09-17).** There is no lock. The canard root attachment must instead carry the steady aerodynamic load (C27) and the launch transient (C28) **directly**, since the canards sit in the airstream from muzzle exit with no stowed state to protect them | C31 user decision 2026-09-17 | DEFINED — closed by architecture |
| C31 | ⚑ In-bore stowage and safety — flush, locked, cannot foul bore or rammer | Chosen | **USER DECISION 2026-09-17 (overriding the assistant's recommendation): the canards are permanently exposed — there is no deployment mechanism.** They are fixed in their flight configuration from manufacture and are exposed inside the bore. In-bore safety therefore becomes a **static clearance problem, not a mechanism problem**: nothing can deploy, so nothing can deploy wrongly, and "cannot foul the bore" is answered by a tolerance budget rather than by a safety argument. **Derived constraints, all from the bore:** (a) bore land diameter **155.0 mm** against a shell body of **154.89 mm** — **0.055 mm radial clearance**, so the body already fills the bore, and the canards must live in the annulus *forward of the ogive* where the shell is only **Ø97.9 mm** at the kit's station; (b) with **2 mm/side engineering clearance** the canard tips cap at **Ø151 mm** → **exposed span 26.5 mm**; (c) **the span cap is expensive because chord does not buy the force back** — at 16 kPa, growing the chord from 3 cm to 8 cm (2.7× area, AR 0.88 → 0.33) raises the pair force only from **10.3 N to 12.7 N**; (d) **C8's 18 N therefore closes only above a q floor ≈23 kPa** (≈8 cm chord, 21.2 cm² per canard) and does **not** close at 16 kPa; (e) **the lever that recovers it is the cant angle, not the area** — `ΔC_Mα = 2·F·l/(q·δ·S·d)` is **independent of canard area and C_Nα**, so cant buys force at no stability cost while area buys it at full cost (feeds C2, C3, C4, C5 and risk 22) | User decision 2026-09-17, overriding the assistant's option A (setback-retained deployment). Reference architecture **[ref]** modified: canards permanently exposed, no deployment mechanism, matching the fielded M1156 PGK's four fixed external canards | DEFINED — carries the **span cap** and the **q floor** as derived constraints |
| C32 | Ring axial retention / restraint design | Chosen | | Must survive C26 | OPEN |
| C33 | Damping torque Tad on the ring | Derived | ≈1.8 → 2.1 N·m **[ref]** | Ring aerodynamics | OPEN |
| C34 | Machine topology — radial-flux frameless outrunner | Chosen | **Radial-flux, 3-phase, frameless outrunner** (rotor with magnets on the ring, stator on the body) — **confirmed by the team 2026-09-18.** Frameless is the load-bearing word: it decouples the magnetic machine from the bearing choice, so C22 stays ours. Outrunner puts the magnets at the largest available radius, which is where the torque is. C44 (axial-flux trade) remains open as a P4 mass lever and does not block this. **Annular (2026-09-19): the machine occupies the annulus about F9’s reserved centreline train bore — the ≈79 mm gap diameter is an outer dimension, not a free bore.** | Team proposal 2026-09-18; `03-caa-machine-sizing.md` §4 | DEFINED 2026-09-18 |
| C35 | ⚑ Air gap and the allowable relative deflection under launch load | Derived | **Gap 0.40 mm → relative rotor-to-stator deflection must stay under 0.20 mm at launch, or the rotor grinds the stator — and deriving it splits the requirement in two, which changes who owns it.** **Axial (73.5 kN, C26): *not* a bearing requirement.** C26 already requires that load to bypass the flight bearings, so the axial case is a stiffness spec on C32's launch path and the stator mount: **k ≥ 73.5 kN / 0.20 mm = 367 N/µm.** A machined steel path closes that by three orders of magnitude — a 50 mm path of 1 500 mm² steel is ≈6 000 N/µm — so the axial case is **comfortable provided nothing soft sits in the path**, and that is now a stated design prohibition: no spring washer, elastomer seat or compliant potting layer in the launch path. **Radial (24.5 kN, E2): *this* is a bearing requirement.** With the radial load carried by the flight bearing pair, **k ≥ 24.5 kN / 0.20 mm = 122 N/µm on the pair** — achievable for a 30–50 mm thin-section instrument bearing, but only **with preload**, which is the same knob that sets C13's friction. **So C35 and C13 pull the preload (C23) in opposite directions, and that tension — not the raw deflection figure — is the real content of this criterion. Trade owner assigned 2026-09-18: C23 (bearing preload) — one preload value must satisfy `k_pair ≥ 122 N/µm` and `Tf ≤ 0.10 N·m` simultaneously; resolved at C23, verified by C25 by test and on the R7 rig.** | Derived from C26 (73.5 kN axial, 24.5 kN radial), E1/E2 (15 000 g / 5 000 g) and the 0.40 mm gap; `03-caa-machine-sizing.md` §8 | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional on the CAD load path (C32) and on the preload decision (C23) |
| C36 | Slot / pole combination | Chosen | **12 slots / 14 poles**, concentrated double-layer, `k_w` = 0.933. Chosen for a high cogging period and a 4.74 mm yoke with a 19.6 mm slot — **confirmed by the team 2026-09-18.** The **high cogging period is the reason this combination is safe against C37**: cogging is a position-locked disturbance competing with the 0.17 N·m apogee torque, and a shorter cogging period (lower slot/pole index) puts more wells in a revolution with the ring's inertia the only thing averaging them out. | Sizing §3 | DEFINED 2026-09-18 |
| C37 | ⚑ Cogging-torque budget versus the apogee authority | Derived | Cogging appears as a **position-locked disturbance torque** on the ring. It loads G5/G6 accuracy, the G14 observer, and must stay well below the apogee `Taero` of **0.17 N·m** or the ring can stick in a cogging well exactly where authority is thinnest. Budget to be set against C12. | New | OPEN |
| C38 | Magnet grade, thickness and retention | Chosen | NdFeB, **1.5 mm radial**, **high-coercivity grade (SH/UH)** for the W9 nose temperature. **Retention must be mechanical** — a 5 g magnet at 15 000 g sees ≈736 N of shear, which adhesive will not hold. Sleeve or pockets, and it is a CAD load case. | Sizing §8 | OPEN |
| C39 | Stator lamination grade and iron loss at electrical frequency | Derived | Electrical frequency **2 130 Hz** at p = 7 and 1912 rad/s (3.0–4.3 kHz at p = 10–14). **0.35 mm laminations will not do** — 0.1–0.2 mm laminations or a soft-magnetic-composite stator are required. The iron-loss figure is **not yet trustworthy** and must be calculated before the machine thermal budget can be closed. Pushes pole count *down*. | Sizing §5 | OPEN |
| C40 | Machine mass | Derived | **≈0.81 kg at L = 25 mm; ≈0.97 kg at L = 30 mm** — copper ≈280–340 g, stator iron ≈210–250 g, rotor backiron ≈250–300 g, magnets ≈72–86 g. **Confirmed with the sizing 2026-09-18.** Against a **1.4 kg kit benchmark this is the dominant mass item** and it moves the net retrofit penalty from +0.42 kg toward **+0.7–0.9 kg**, with the CG shift from 2.6 mm to **6–8 mm forward** (risk 33) — which is still ≈0.8 % of projectile length and inside the platform's own envelope spread, so it is a **soft failure, not a budget failure**. **C44 (axial-flux) and C45 (make-versus-buy) are the levers on this number**, and they are the reason to keep both open. | Sizing §4, §8 | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional on the CAD mass properties (C21's route) |
| C41 | ⚑ Bus voltage and `Kv` | Chosen | **Bus revised 2026-09-19 (H8/H12): ≈150–200 V, 180 V nominal; `Kv` = 0.209 V/(rad/s) LL and `Kt` = 0.362 N·m/A rms stand unchanged** (convention `Kt = √3·Kv`). The original sizing took **400 V** — confirmed by the team 2026-09-18 — and the reasoning that made either choice admissible is worth keeping, because it is exactly what H8 then used: **the bus is a *free* choice, since copper loss is identical at every voltage because `R ∝ Kt²` at fixed slot area** — so it was settled on partial-discharge grounds at altitude, not on efficiency (1.4 kV in a small potted assembly is a real risk for zero thermal gain; the reference's 0.749 implies a 1.4 kV machine and a 1.4 kV rectifier). **H8 exercised that freedom the other way**, setting the bus below the flight-minimum EMF — E_min = 0.209 × 1107.5 rad/s ≈ **231 V** — so the four-quadrant stage never crosses the buck↔boost boundary, at the price of bus-side current which H8 accepted. The winding, `Kt` and `R` were deliberately left alone (C17), so the machine sizing stands. **This row resolves G7 — and *refutes* its recorded basis rather than confirming it:** the back-EMF ceiling is `V_bus/Kv`, the body-spin scale, which is 61× the ±31.42 rad/s ring limit, so the rate limit is not a bus consequence at all. The genuine bus condition is that the machine reaches `E = V_bus` at launch spin, i.e. at peak load — handed to W8/C17 and closed there by H12. | Sizing §6; **bus revision from H8 (2026-09-19) and H12 (180 V nominal)** | **DEFINED 2026-09-18, bus value revised 2026-09-19 — resolves G7 and now carries the H8 revision** |
| C42 | ⚑ Continuous generated power — the energy that must be dissipated | Derived | **`P = Taero × ω_body` peaks at 2.92 kW at launch and integrates to ≈121 kJ over the flight.** C20's 1.2 kW rating is exceeded **×2.4**. The bench rig ran body spin 21.6× slower, so the same torque produced 21.6× less power — **the rig's speed scaling masked a kilowatt-class problem**, the second instance of that trap in this project. This is what W7 must dissipate and it cannot be stored. **Confirmed 2026-09-18 as the load W7 and W12 shall be sized to** — and it is a *design* quantity, not an accident: it is the price of the de-spun architecture, and the trade against a servo-actuated canard kit (no such load at all) belongs in the submission rather than in a footnote | Sizing §7; feeds W7 | DERIVED — **confirmed as the authority for W7/W12**; magnitude still provisional on the aero set (risk 8) |
| C43 | ⚑ Cant-angle ↔ roll-torque ↔ dumped-power coupling | Derived | Raising cant buys guidance force (risk 25's primary mitigation) **and raises `Taero` and the dumped power in proportion**: 10° → 16° gives +60 % force and +60 % dissipation (2.92 → 4.67 kW). **Cant angle is not free**, and the register previously treated it as such. Trades against C8, C9, C42. **Confirmed 2026-09-18: this coupling is binding on the C2/C3 decision** — raising cant to recover C8's force at the q floor (risk 25) buys the force and the dissipation together, so C2/C3 cannot be settled without costing both, and a cant choice that clears C8 but overruns W7's thermal budget is not a solution | Sizing §7 | DERIVED — **confirmed; binding on C2/C3** |
| C44 | Radial-flux versus axial-flux trade | Chosen | Not yet traded. The annulus is short and wide (≤95.25 mm above the seat, Ø92 mm), which is the case where a **pancake axial-flux** machine can win on magnet area. The radial build also competes with the bearing and structure inside the same annulus — see risk 27. | New | OPEN |
| C45 | Make versus buy — standard frameless kit or bespoke stack | Chosen | Frameless kits exist in standard diameters. A standard frame size is dramatically cheaper than a bespoke lamination stack, and the PS premise is low cost. Check availability **before** committing to a custom winding. | New | OPEN |

## F — Fuze

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Modes and setting ranges (proximity / time / impact) | Given / Chosen | **Claimed — all three PS modes, with a full setting envelope.** Proximity: settable burst height **2–15 m**, nominal default ≈7 m. Time: **0.1–199.9 s** at 0.1 s resolution. Impact: **superquick / short delay (≈0.05 s) / graze**, selectable. All configurable before flight over the inductive gun-line setter (F11). **Adopted provision — and corrected 2026-09-19 to *adopted and credited* rather than claimed: the time setting is re-synchronised in flight.** AOP-22 Annex E.16 registers the **XM8, "FUZE, COURSE-CORRECTION, ELECTRONIC TIME FUZE"**, and states outright: *"In course correction mode, the time is also corrected in flight to improve accuracy."* A NATO-registered fielded course-correction fuze therefore already does this, so it is a target we are meeting and not an invention of this kit. **What remains ours is narrower and must be stated as such:** the re-synchronisation is driven by *our own continuous nav solution* rather than by a ballistic or firing-table correction, and it keeps the bounded one-way gated form described here. In effect, the guided round's airburst tracks its *actual* time of flight; the fuze stays autonomous and safety-critical — absent a correction it functions on its pre-set time, and corrections are one-way, bounded and gated on arming (risk 26). The mode *set* still exceeds the US fielded benchmark, whose fuze is PD + proximity only (but see F16's note on the US kit's withheld-detonation behaviour) | PS (three-mode fuze) + M2 (mode set and terminal-effect retention, DEFINED). Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). **Assumptions named:** the 2–15 m band from 155 mm HE optimum height-of-burst practice (~5–10 m for personnel / light materiel, around a 7 m default); the 199.9 s envelope from class electronic-time-fuze setting practice; ≈0.05 s delay from class short-delay practice. Confirm all three against YIL's in-service fuze and setter data post-selection. Benchmark: M1156 PGK is PD + proximity — `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §4, S4 | DEFINED — ranges class-based; re-confirm against YIL's in-service fuze post-selection |
| F2 | Proximity sensor technology and operating frequency | Chosen | **Claimed — RF Doppler proximity sensing, 24 GHz class (ISM band).** Body-mounted in the nose tip ahead of the canard ring, radiating through a **radome**; no optical window anywhere on the kit. Delivers burst height inside F1's 2–15 m envelope. **Stated requirement carried out of this decision: the detection scheme shall reject the spin modulation of the ground return** — the antenna turns with the body at **176.3–304.4 Hz** [S6], so a threshold that ignores the rotation bursts the round at the wrong height. The mechanism (amplitude/threshold Doppler versus FM-CW ranging) is fixed at F3 together with the accuracy figure. Frequency rationale (F15): 24 GHz is spectrally clear of GNSS (1.2 / 1.5 GHz) and of the alternator's electrical content — 7 pole pairs × up to 304.4 rev/s ≈ **2.1 kHz** — so the remaining coexistence exposure is converter **switching harmonics**, not the radar itself. COTS availability at 24 GHz is the cost argument (K4); the class norm for artillery is RF proximity | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Frequency band and radome arrangement **PROPOSED** — final frequency set with F15 and the nose layout. Rejected: Ka-band ≈35 GHz (cost and atmospheric attenuation against an accuracy need not yet demonstrated), IR/optical (window in the hottest, most bore-abraded surface; weather- and obscurant-sensitive), nav-triggered airburst (makes a safety-critical function depend on the nav solution, contradicting F1's autonomy provision) | DEFINED — technology and band fixed; frequency and radome PROPOSED |
| F3 | Burst height range and accuracy | Chosen | | | OPEN |
| F4 | Time mode accuracy and resolution | Chosen | | Crystal reference | OPEN |
| F5 | Impact delay options (superquick / delay / graze) | Chosen | | | OPEN |
| F6 | Safe-and-arm type (out-of-line rotor) | Chosen | **Claimed — MIL-STD-1316 out-of-line rotor, released by two independent mechanical environment stimuli (setback and spin) with the electronics' arm command as a third, independent condition.** The rotor is driven into line by its own spring/centrifugal force; the electrics enable, they do not actuate. Stimuli are the two the register already owns: **15 000 g axial setback [E1]** and **176.3–304.4 Hz spin [S6]**, so F7's thresholds have named physical anchors rather than invented ones. **The arming sequence shall be such that the rotor cannot complete its travel while setback is sustained** — so no function is possible in the bore **by construction rather than by timer**, which is the form the "no in-bore function" requirement takes in this architecture. **Fail-safe direction:** dead electronics or an absent stimulus gives a dud, never a premature burst; a misfire (no setback) leaves it safe with **no abort logic required**; and because the ring is de-spun, the ring's own relative motion cannot supply a setback stimulus. Carries volume in the Ø50.8 mm well stack (P12) | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Governing standard MIL-STD-1316 (cited at F13). Stimulus anchors from E1 (15 000 g, **[ref]**) and S6 (176.3–304.4 Hz, confirmed). Downstream: F7 sets the thresholds, F8 the arming distance. **Coupling recorded:** F6/F8 are not a pure safety question — G3's guided-phase opening edge is max(arming distance, nav convergence), and the M7 authority margin is already 1.11:1 and worse under the gate (risk 10), so option A protects that budget by keeping the arming distance short. Rejected: single-stimulus rotor with electrical second mechanism (B) — puts half the safety case on the electronics and the reserve battery against MIL-STD-1316's two-independent-mechanism rule; electrically actuated rotor (C) — arming then depends on the battery being alive and the electronics healthy at the right instant, needs abort logic, and adds failure modes in the subsystem a reviewer probes hardest | DEFINED — type fixed; thresholds and distance to F7 / F8 |
| F7 | Arming interlock thresholds (setback and spin) | Derived | | Must not arm in bore | OPEN |
| F8 | Arming distance after muzzle exit | Chosen | | | OPEN |
| F9 | Explosive train, detonator type, output | Chosen | **Reserved constraint (added 2026-09-19): the train runs on the kit’s axis, and everything else is annular around it.** The kit replaces the fuze, so whatever the detonator/output arrangement turns out to be, the initiation path must reach the shell’s booster **on the centreline at the well bottom** — which puts the machine, the dump stage, the buffer, the boards and the harness all in the **annulus**, with F6’s out-of-line interruption on that axis. The predecessor study in this project enforces exactly this as a CAD rule — a **≈Ø30 mm through-bore reserved for the full length of the fuze-well and machine sections**, with the fuze stack built as rings about it — and the constraint follows here from the well geometry (P6, P12) independently of that study. **No criterion owned it until now**, and it is the first thing the P4 CAD will hit: layout decisions already taken in C34 (machine), W12 (dump), W4 (buffer) and H5 (boards) all assume space that this removes from the centreline. | YIL / standard components; centreline-train convention from standard fuze practice and the predecessor study’s CAD (2026-09-19) | OPEN — **the reservation is a stated constraint; the train components remain YIL’s** |
| F10 | ⚑ Dud rate target | Chosen | **The target must now carry a deliberate contribution: F16’s predicted-miss failsafe withholds function on a predicted miss beyond 150 m, so the achieved function rate is below one by design.** | Safety and effectiveness; F16 | OPEN — **scope modified by F16, 2026-09-20** |
| F11 | Setter interface (inductive) and protocol | Given / Chosen | **PARAMETRIC — the standard gun-line interface, not a new one, and now a *named* standard.** The 155 mm class is set inductively on the gun line: the fielded M1156 PGK is set with the Enhanced Portable Inductive Artillery Fuze Setter, so an inductive interface is the class norm and the kit shall not require new gun-line equipment. **The governing authority is NATO AOP-22 Edition B Version 1 (May 2018), *Design Criteria and Test Methods for Inductive Setting of Large Calibre Projectile Fuzes*, supporting STANAG 4369 / AOP-4369** — which fixes the numbers rather than leaving them generic: carrier **100 ± 0.01 kHz**; forward-message bit rate **1 000 ± 10 bit/s**; mark/space rise and fall **≤ 50 µs**; fuze message window **275 ± 5 ms**; power-up period 1–1 000 ms; and a carrier inducing **36.2 to 48.2 V peak-to-peak** into a standard fuze receive coil. AOP-22 §3.3 also makes the **transmit–repeat–compare read-back the standard's own error-detection mechanism** (so F12's read-back is compliance, not invention), §3.6 defines a command mode including a safe-condition setting, and §3.6(c) permits the fuze to **calibrate its time base against the setter's precision time base** — an external anchor for H6/N15. **The registered-fuze registry in Annex E matters to us in its own right:** it is the list of fuzes a setter must already talk to, and it contains two *course-correction* fuzes (XM7, XM8) that each use **two** message windows | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Standard: NATO AOP-22 Ed B Ver 1, May 2018 (pp. 3-1 to 3-4, Annexes A–E); fielded setter practice: EPIAFS (NDIA Fuze Conference 2006); class practice and the M1156 PGK setting procedure (public); `02-reference-155mm-model.md` §7. Source register F1, F7, F9 | PARAMETRIC — **standard named and the interface fully specified externally; protocol detail still confirmed against YIL's in-service setter post-selection.** **The window figures expose risk 44: F12's package contents are now countable and must be counted** |
| F12 | Setter data package contents (mode, time of flight, target coordinates, met, muzzle velocity) | Chosen | **Claimed — the full mission package, delivered through the in-service inductive setter, with a verified read-back.** Contents: (1) fuze mode and its parameters (burst-height gate, time, impact delay) [F1]; (2) **target coordinates** — the input IPP cannot work without; (3) **met** data; (4) charge / muzzle-velocity state; (5) time reference (feeds both the F1 in-flight re-sync and GNSS); (6) **GNSS ephemeris and keys** — the fielded-kit precedent, and the means by which a fast fix is possible under spin (N3); (7) round ID for logging [R10]. **Two provisions:** the setter **reads back what it wrote** and the crew gets a **go/no-go before the round is loaded** (this is AOP-22 §3.3's own error-detection mechanism — so it is compliance rather than a design feature, and it should be described as such), and the uploaded target is **checked against the firing solution** — a target outside the charge/range envelope is detectable before firing rather than after, and *that* provision is ours. Delivery is via F11's in-service inductive interface, so **the setter's data capacity is a named item in F11's post-selection confirmation**; if it proves too small the fallback is split programming (option B), not a redesign. **Open exposure (risk 44, new 2026-09-19):** AOP-22 fixes the forward window at **275 ± 5 ms at 1 000 ± 10 bit/s ≈ 275 bits**, and the two registered course-correction fuzes use **two** windows — so ≈550 bits is the standard's own budget for a whole CCF package, and this list has never been counted against it. Ephemeris alone is of that order. **H11 owns the bit budget**; the first fixes available are ageing the ephemeris to almanac-only and shortening the key validity | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A). Closes the first half of risk 13 — G1 named target coordinates plus met/muzzle-velocity data in the fire-control package as a hard dependency. The target-location term itself stays **outside** the CEP claim per M5 and is published as a sensitivity (risk 9). New failure path recorded as risk 28. Standard context: AOP-22 §3.3 (read-back), §3.6 (command mode and rejection criteria), Annex E (XM7/XM8 two-window course-correction fuzes). Rejected: split programming (B) — two programming steps create a mission/round mismatch mode and weaken F11's "no new gun-line equipment" premise; mode-and-time only (C) — breaks G1 and wastes the G4 allocation, a conflict rather than a choice | DEFINED — package fixed; **setter capacity confirmed post-selection (F11) and the package's bit budget derived at H11 (risk 44)** |
| F13 | Safety standards compliance set | Given | MIL-STD-1316, STANAG 4369, AOP-39 | Cite rather than invent | OPEN |
| F14 | Storage life / shelf life | Given / Chosen | | | OPEN |
| F15 | ⚑ EMI coexistence: proximity sensor vs alternator switching vs magnetometer | Derived | **Derived — and answerable because both magnetic disturbances are functions of quantities the kit already measures.** Coupling paths quantified: the ring's magnets pass a body-fixed point at **7 pole pairs × 176.3–304.4 Hz = 1 234–2 131 Hz**; the coil current produces a quasi-DC field tracking flight phase (≈10 A launch → 5 A apogee → 6–7 A descent); the converter's switching edges are the one broadband source; and the 24 GHz radar is ~16× above L-band with no in-band victim. **Claimed allocations and mechanisms:** (1) magnetometer band **≤ 50 Hz**, the rotating term rejected by a **coherent notch locked to the measured relative rotation** (N9/N10 measures it anyway) rather than a blind filter, and the current-proportional term **compensated in the in-flight calibration model as a function of measured coil current** — which is exactly what G6's in-flight-calibration requirement demands; (2) converter switching **≥ 100 kHz with controlled edge rate and L-band attenuation to the GNSS threshold**, the only broadband path, verified under E8; (3) radar and machine separated by station (nose tip versus ring station) behind their own ground planes. **Derived negative result: the magnetometer case cannot be solved spatially** — interference and measurand share the same band *and* the same space, so shielding cannot remove one without the other, which is why the answer is spectral and temporal rather than shielding-first. **Cost: no extra hardware** — a magnetometer of adequate bandwidth plus signals already required (relative speed, coil current) — which matters because the nose has no volume to spare (risk 27) | Derived from S6 (176.3–304.4 Hz spin), C16 (7 pole pairs), the C9/C18 current profile **[ref]**, G6 (in-flight calibration requirement) and N9/N10 (relative-speed measurement). Confirmed by the user 2026-09-17. Verification: rig run with the alternator at flight-representative speed (R7), plus E8 / MIL-STD-461. **Priced fallback if the magnetometer proves unworkable:** delete it and take the earth reference from pre-launch alignment plus gyrocompassing — reopens G6, and G6's decision log prices that at ring angle 15.3 m, guidance term 21.0 m, **CEP 27.9 m (≈7 % margin, against 20 %)** | DERIVED — confirmed; the band allocations are Claimed requirements |

| F16 | ⚑ **Predicted-miss failsafe: does the fuze withhold function when the predicted impact lies far from the loaded target?** | Chosen | **DEFINED 2026-09-20 — the fuze withholds function on a predicted miss, and the mechanism is the arming logic the design already has.** The kit inhibits when the predicted impact is still more than **150 m** from the loaded target at the point the guidance can no longer change the outcome — equivalently, on the first cycle in which the predicted miss exceeds the threshold **and** the correction authority remaining (from the current dynamic pressure and the time to go) is no longer sufficient to bring it inside. The inhibit **latches and is irrevocable.** **150 m is the class datum, adopted rather than invented** — the radius the fielded kit in this class publishes — and it is not in tension with our own claim, because a round predicted to land beyond it has already left the effect envelope we are claiming. **The decision time is therefore not a clock: it is the moment the residual stops shrinking**, which is the latest point the authority allows and the one at which G4’s propagation error is smallest. **The arming interaction is the finding — the failsafe adds no mechanism.** F6 already requires the electrical arm command as a third, independent condition on top of setback and spin, so **the failsafe is the withholding of that command**: the rotor never goes in line, the round is a dud rather than a burst, and the failsafe can therefore only ever make the round safer and can never cause function. It rides the same bounded, one-way, arming-gated path the F1 time correction already uses (H10), so F1’s autonomy provision is preserved — the fuze still arms and functions on its own, and the veto is bounded, one-way and absent by default. **Cost, stated rather than hidden (risk 40):** a prediction error can inhibit a round that would have landed acceptably; the exposure is bounded by the propagation error over the *remaining* flight, which is exactly why the decision is taken as late as the authority allows. **Carried to F10:** the failsafe deliberately creates duds, so the dud-rate target must carry its contribution rather than assume a function rate of one | User decision 2026-09-20 (option A of the three priced forms). Mechanism standard-supported: AOP-22 §3.6’s safe-condition command mode, with safety-unacceptable bit patterns among the fuze’s own rejection criteria. Threshold radius from the fielded class figure. Feeds F10 (dud rate), F1 (autonomy provision), F6 (arming gate), H10 (one-way path), R4 (fault response) | **DEFINED — the user’s decision, 2026-09-20**; the latch logic and the arm-command interface are the design outputs |

## N — Navigation & sensing

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | ⚑ Navigation accuracy at impact (1σ, m) | Derived | **Derived — the 5 m allocation now has a bottom-up basis and closes with ≈26 % margin.** RSS of three terms: GNSS position at impact **3.0 m** (multi-constellation, single-frequency, spin-tolerant antenna, spin-induced C/N₀ loss already included); IMU and filter residual **2.0 m** (MEMS-grade IMU with spin removed by the ring mount, continuous GNSS aiding over ≈90 s); time-sync and lever-arm residual **1.0 m** (GNSS time, sub-millisecond). **RSS = √(9 + 4 + 1) = 3.7 m against G4's 5 m allocation.** **Two derived consequences.** (1) **This budget is a statement about GNSS availability, not inertial quality:** with aiding the IMU carries attitude and gap-filling only; unaided, an attitude error of just **0.1°** mis-resolving a 5 g drag deceleration gives ≈0.09 m/s², which over 50 s is **≈110 m** — two orders of magnitude worse than the allocation. **So the 5 m holds conditional on GNSS, and N4 must bound the outage tolerance to protect it** — a new dependency carried to N4. (2) **The 2 m IMU term is only possible because the IMU sits on the de-spun ring** — a body-fixed IMU cannot separate 1600 rad/s of spin from attitude motion — so N1's credibility rests directly on N6. **Leverage recorded:** nav 5 → 10 m moves the CEP from 23.9 m to **26.0 m** and the margin from 20 % to 13 % | Derived from G4 (the 5 m top-down allocation) with a named assumption set: the GNSS term is verified by **N3** (antenna) and R5, the IMU term by **N8** (bias, ARW) and **N6** (ring mount — **closed 2026-09-18**), the timing term by N15. Confirmed by the user 2026-09-17. Spin values from S6 (176.3–304.4 Hz) | DERIVED — provisional on **N3 and N8**; **N6 closed 2026-09-18**, so the 2.0 m term's mount dependency is satisfied |
| N2 | GNSS constellations and update rate | Chosen | | | OPEN |
| N3 | ⚑ GNSS antenna strategy under spin (spin-tolerant pattern / array / ring-mounted) | Chosen | **Claimed — a rotationally symmetric aperture on the spin axis, its elements combined rather than switched.** The projectile spins about its own axis and the antenna sits on it, so the combined pattern is **invariant under rotation: the spin produces no amplitude or phase modulation by symmetry**, and there is nothing at 176.3–304.4 Hz to track out. A standard receiver and a normal 5–15 Hz loop therefore suffice — no receiver redesign. Residual modulation is the **coning / yaw precession**, which is slow, plus any antenna or radome asymmetry. **Consequences carried:** the aperture shares the kit's axis with the 24 GHz radar front end [F2] and the **radome must pass both L1 and 24 GHz** — a named item in risk 27's packing budget, with the spectral separation already established by F15. **Acquisition becomes a requirement rather than a goal:** the fix must complete in the opening seconds of flight, because G3's guided-phase edge is max(arming distance, nav convergence) and the window behind it is spent against a 1.11:1 authority margin (risks 10, 25) — **F12's pre-loaded ephemeris and keys are the enabler**. **Fallback carried (option C):** if the residual coning modulation proves worse than expected, feed the **measured spin phase** (N9/N10) into the receiver and demodulate deterministically — at the cost of a custom receiver and a timing path into the RF front end, i.e. cost against K4 | Method choice: user 2026-09-17 (option A, with C as the stated fallback). Spin values from S6 (176.3–304.4 Hz). N1's **3.0 m** GNSS term is the requirement this row must deliver. Gain, pattern symmetry and acquisition time are **PROPOSED** pending the radome layout and N2. **Rejected option B (ring-mounted aperture)** — refuted by our own architecture: **the ring angle is the guidance command channel (G2)**, so an antenna needing a particular attitude would fight the guidance law; add the ring's occupied volume (canards, magnets, coils), the lossy RF path across the bearing and C13's friction budget, and B spends guidance authority to solve a receiver problem | DEFINED — strategy fixed; gain, pattern and acquisition time PROPOSED |
| N4 | GNSS outage tolerance | Chosen | | Graceful degradation | OPEN |
| N5 | Anti-jam requirement | Chosen | | **No longer a future upgrade — X17 (2026-09-18) makes anti-jam GNSS the primary GPS-denied answer.** This row therefore sets the requirement level: null depth, jamming-to-signal margin and bandwidth for N3's multi-element aperture, against the R4 rule that no mode may be worse than unguided | OPEN — promoted to baseline by X17 |
| N6 | IMU mounting location (de-spun ring) | Chosen | **Claimed — the IMU rides the de-spun ring, with the body-side spin channel the design already needs (option C).** The ring is de-spun from the body's **1107–1912 rad/s**, so the inertial measurement is taken with the spin removed **mechanically rather than numerically**: the unit sees the airframe's coning motion (a few rad/s transverse; nutation ≈13–27 Hz) plus its own slew about the roll axis, limited to **±31.42 rad/s** (G7). The body-side spin-rate channel (**N9/N10**) that feeds the G15 limit laws and the F15 coherent notch is **not extra hardware — it is required regardless**, which is what makes option C the ring mount *plus* a channel rather than a compromise. **Consequences carried:** power and data cross the ring–body interface (contactless preferred — risk 34; fixed with N10/C22); the ring pickoff measures ring-to-body angle while N6 removes the spin from the inertial signal. **This is the criterion slide 3's architectural claim rests on.** **Added 2026-09-19 — the mount is a procurement decision, not only a noise decision.** A body-fixed IMU on this airframe must resolve **92 880 °/s** on the roll axis (S6: 258.1 rev/s at 15 484 rpm). **No COTS part exists in that class** — the industrial and tactical ceiling is ±2 000–4 000 °/s, and the predecessor study in this project ran a ±4 000 °/s part saturated **22×** through the ascent and had to carry the roll reference on a software anchor instead. A gun-hard specialist part reaches the class only in a special high-rate variant (≥ **±25 000 °/s** on the roll axis) and still saturates **≈3.7×** during the spin phase — and it is export-controlled. **So the ring mount is what makes a COTS MEMS IMU possible at all:** N7's ±2 000 °/s is a *consequence* of N6 rather than an independent claim, and the alternative to N6 is not “a better gyro” but “a specialist part plus a software roll anchor”. That is the sentence to carry on slide 3 | User choice 2026-09-18 (option C, the assistant's recommendation). Rejected (a) ring mount alone — leaves the spin channel implicit although G15 and F15 both require it; rejected (b) body-fixed IMU with the spin handled in the filter — the measurement is dominated by the 176–304 Hz spin (a 0.1 % scale-factor error on ≈1900 rad/s is ≈2 rad/s of phantom rate, orders above the nav need) and N1's own sensitivity shows the line must be re-cut (nav 5 → 10 m → CEP 26.0 m, margin 13 %) | DEFINED 2026-09-18 — **back-fills N1's 2.0 m term** |
| N7 | IMU rate range required | Derived | **±1,800 °/s on the roll (ring-slew) axis and ±300–600 °/s transverse — a 3-axis ±2,000 °/s unit with ≥100 Hz bandwidth.** With the spin removed by the ring mount (N6), the roll axis sees only the ring's own slew, whose limit is **±31.42 rad/s = ±1,800 °/s** (G7); the transverse axes see the airframe's coning — S4's ≤3° yaw at the nutation rate (**≈13–27 Hz, fixed by P3's aero set**) is ≈4–9 rad/s, carried to ±300–600 °/s with margin — and ≥100 Hz bandwidth keeps the nutation in the measurement rather than aliasing it. **Cost lever kept open: if the roll rate is taken from the N10 pickoff instead, the IMU itself can drop to a ±500 °/s part** | Derived from N6 (ring mount), G7 (±31.42 rad/s slew limit), S4 (≤3° yaw) | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional on the P3 nutation rate |
| N8 | IMU bias stability / angular random walk | Derived | **ARW ≤ 1 °/√h and bias instability ≤ 5 °/h — tactical/industrial-grade MEMS, and the spec is generous rather than tight.** With continuous GNSS aiding (5–15 Hz, N2/N3) the IMU supplies attitude observability and gap-filling, not dead-reckoning: at ARW 1 °/√h a 2 s coast contributes ≈0.02° of attitude, and at 5 °/h the bias drift over 10 s is ≈0.014° — both negligible against N1's 2.0 m term, so what sets the part is **grade availability in the SWaP-C lane (K4), not the filter.** **The unaided case is not a navigation mode at all** — N1's ≈110 m per 0.1° of attitude error over 50 s — so the outage-coast bound is **N4's**, and N8 must not be read as supplying it. Confirmed by R5's covariance | Derived from N1 (2.0 m IMU/filter term) and G3's window; outage bound is N4's | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional (R5's filter covariance; N4's outage bound) |
| N9 | Spin rate measurement range | Given | 1600 → 1050 rad/s **[ref]** | Flight envelope | OPEN |
| N10 | Spin rate sensor type | Chosen | | Magnetic / optical pickoff | OPEN |
| N11 | Barometric altitude accuracy | Derived | | Contributes to aero model density — the density reference is the **P3 environment model, named 2026-09-19 (decision log)**; the sensor itself stays OPEN | OPEN |
| N12 | Magnetometer accuracy, calibration and disturbance rejection | Derived | | Absolute ring angle (G6) | OPEN |
| N13 | Sensor shock rating | Given | 15 000 g axial / 5 000 g radial **[ref]** | E1, E2 | OPEN |
| N14 | Navigation filter type (EKF / UKF) and state vector | Chosen | | | OPEN |
| N15 | Time synchronisation across sensors | Derived | **DERIVED — the kit keeps one hardware time base and every sample is stamped when it is converted; GNSS labels the epoch and never steers the clock. Closes at H6 (option A, user decision 2026-09-19).** The requirement: **mission-side timestamp uncertainty ≤ 100 µs (1σ)** — against N1's 1.0 m time/lever-arm term (≈ 1.1 ms at ≈ 900 m/s) — met by conversion-time stamping against a shared 32-bit counter in D2 with a TCXO reference; the loop's sampling instants carry no software jitter by construction (H1) and its HRTIM is locked to the same clock source with a one-time phase measurement. **The one place the rule is not automatic is the ring–body joint:** the IMU must stamp on the ring, or the link's delay must be measured and bounded (H7 owns it; risk 34 extended) | Settled at H6 — the architecture is H6's, and the number follows from N1's allocation. ## W — Power & thermal

| ID | Criterion | Type | Value | Basis / source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | Energy budget by phase (safe state, boost, midcourse, terminal) | Derived | **DERIVED 2026-09-18 — four phases, and the alternator supplies from t = 0** (the ring is de-spun, so relative speed equals the full body spin at launch). *Pre-launch / safe state:* reserve battery only (W5) — arming logic and setter read-back, **≲1 W**; capacity is W5's. *Boost (0–10 s):* generation 2.9 → 2.0 kW against ≈12 W of demand. *Midcourse (10–60 s):* generation 1.2 kW → **0.24 kW at apogee (t ≈ 41 s)** → 1.4 kW, demand ≈10 W. *Terminal (60–86 s):* generation 1.4 → 2.2 kW, demand ≈11 W with the radar active. **In-flight demand totals ≈0.9 kJ against 121 kJ generated (C42)** — the surplus is the dump's (W7), and the W4 buffer covers transitions and bursts, never the integral | Derived from C42's `P(t)` profile, the W4 unit-load inventory and W5's boundary | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional on the P4 electronics load estimate |
| W2 | Peak electrical power | Derived | **DERIVED 2026-09-18 — ≈12 W of continuous demand, with two peak paths that are not the avionics.** (1) **Bus throughput peaks at the dump: 2.92 kW at launch** (C42/W12) — that is what the bus and the dump stage must carry. (2) **Demand peaks at a motoring burst: ≈2–5 kW for tens of ms**, sourced from the W4 supercapacitor buffer behind a bidirectional interface, not from instant generation. The avionics themselves never exceed ≈12 W | Derived from C42, W4 and the unit-load inventory | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional (P4 electronics load) |
| W3 | Average electrical power | Derived | **DERIVED 2026-09-18 — ≈10 W of electrical demand against ≈1.34 kW through the bus.** The avionics and fuze average ≈10 W (≈0.9 kJ per flight); the bus average is dominated by the dump, which must shed 121 kJ / 90 s = **1.34 kW** (W7). The factor of ≈130 between the two is the criterion's real headline: **the electrical demand is negligible; the thermal load is everything** | Derived from C42, W4 and the unit-load inventory | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — provisional (P4 electronics load; C39 iron loss) |
| W4 | Generation capability versus demand | Derived | **DERIVED 2026-09-18 — the demand never binds; the surplus is the design problem, and it now has both a dump and a buffer.** Demand inventory (unit loads, verified in P4): flight processor + FPGA 5 W peak / 4 avg; GNSS 2 / 2; IMU, magnetometer and ring pickoff (ring-side, across the rotating joint) 1.5 / 1.0; 24 GHz front end 1.5 / 0.5 (duty-cycled); fuze logic, timer, S&A drive and setter 1.0 / 0.5; dump gate drive 1.0; conversion losses (≈85 %) 1.5 / 1.2 → **≈12 W peak, ≈10 W average, ≈0.9 kJ per flight.** Generation (C42): **2.92 kW peak / 121 kJ; 0.24 kW floor at apogee.** **Result: worst-case in-flight generation is ≈20× the entire electrical demand — the avionics are ≈0.8 % of the energy — so W4's content is the routing statement, not adequacy.** **Decision (option A + a supercapacitor buffer, user 2026-09-18):** one dump-regulated 400 V bus, avionics on a regulated DC/DC, **plus a supercapacitor bank behind a bidirectional interface** carrying three duties: **(1) the fireset reservoir** — ≥ 1 J dedicated and isolated, **charged only under the arming interlock (F6/F7)** (the S&A rotor is mechanically driven, so this is the fuze's only electrical firing duty; the initiator class is F9's, and 1 J covers EFI-class); **(2) avionics and fuze hold-up** — ≥ 2 s at ≈ 12 W = **24 J**, which also rides through the launch-condition converter degeneracy G7 handed to W8/C17; **(3) actuator motoring bursts** — `P = T × ω_rel` is **≈ 2–5 kW for tens of ms** on a 30° slew at flight relative speed, **≈ 100–150 J throughput per slew** and net ≈ zero over the flight (the ring's own kinetic energy is only 0.6 J, so this is a *power* buffer, not an energy store). **Sizing: ≈ 150–200 J usable, ≈ 50–150 g** at a low-voltage rail — a 400 V bank would need ≈ 2.5 mF and hundreds of grams, rejected on mass. **Scope honest: duty (3) is a capability addition, not a survival requirement** — the machine's braking range already covers both slew senses, and motoring only adds authority in the sense that adds to the aero couple; duties (1) and (2) are requirements. Boundary: W5's reserve battery still carries the pre-launch safe state; this buffer is in-flight. **Claim discipline: "no in-flight battery" survives — a supercapacitor is not a battery — but the deck sentence must become "the energy comes from the alternator; a supercapacitor buffer carries the fireset reservoir, the avionics hold-up and the actuator bursts."** | C20, C42, W1–W3, W5, W7, W8, W12, C9, F6, F7, F9, G7, P9, risk 33, risk 35. Method: derived from C42 and the unit-load inventory; user decision 2026-09-18 (option A + supercapacitor) | DERIVED 2026-09-18 — **confirmed by the team with the supercapacitor addition**; provisional on C39 (iron loss) and the P4 electronics load estimate |
| W5 | Reserve battery chemistry, activation method, capacity | Chosen | **Scope fixed 2026-09-18 with W4:** the reserve battery carries the **pre-launch / safe-state load only** — arming logic and setter read-back, ≲1 W — and hands over at muzzle exit, where the machine already sees full body spin. **In-flight hold-up, the fireset reservoir and the actuator bursts are the W4 supercapacitor buffer's, not the battery's.** Chemistry, activation and capacity remain to be chosen | No power before launch = safety enabler; boundary with W4 (supercapacitor) settled 2026-09-18 | OPEN — scope fixed |
| W6 | Reserve battery shelf life | Given | | | OPEN |
| W7 | ⚑ Energy dissipation path for control power (no load bank in flight) | Chosen | **DERIVED 2026-09-18 — and the answer is not a radiator, it is a heat sink the kit does not own.** The load is **121 kJ in ≤90 s at a peak of 2.92 kW** (C42). Three routes were priced rather than assumed: **(1) storage — excluded:** 121 kJ at a 400 V bus needs ≈1.5 F, which neither fits the envelope nor survives 15 000 g; **(2) the kit's own thermal mass — insufficient:** 121 kJ into the kit's ≈1.5–2.0 kg of metal at c_p ≈ 0.45 kJ/(kg·K) is a **134–179 K rise**, and holding it to a 100 K rise would need **≈2.7 kg** — more than twice the entire P9 mass budget; **(3) convective rejection to the airstream — a quarter of it, and not at the peak:** the kit's ≈0.05 m² of surface at h ≈ 100 W/(m²·K) against a recovery-temperature-limited ΔT ≈ 70 K rejects **≈350 W**, which is **≈26 % of the 90 s integral but only 12 % of the peak**. **The path that closes is conduction into the ≈45 kg shell body through the fuze-well thread: 121 kJ into 45 kg of steel is a 5.8 K rise**, so the shell is effectively an infinite sink and the requirement becomes an **interface** requirement rather than a surface-area one. **Specification: an actively controlled resistive dump stage sized by C42 (2.92 kW peak, 121 kJ total), thermally bonded to the stator mount and conduction-coupled to the fuze-well threads, with the kit-to-shell interface required to carry the peak at ≤ 50 K (≥ 58 W/K).** **Refined 2026-09-18 (P6 check): 58 W/K is the *peak* figure; the flight average is ≥ 20–27 W/K, and the bare thread + seat joint delivers only ≈3–16 W/K (central ≈ 8) — so the interface is *designed* (well bore as the high-area path + a conductance-specified joint) and verified on the rig, not inherited from the thread.** | C42, C20, C43, W1, W9, W10, risk 27, risk 32. Method: derived from C42 with the stated assumptions (kit metal mass 1.5–2.0 kg, c_p 0.45 kJ/(kg·K), h 100 W/(m²·K), ΔT 70 K, shell 45 kg) | DERIVED — **confirmed by the team 2026-09-18.** The largest input is a platform interaction the kit does not own (the thread's thermal conductance), so it is carried as a **declared interface requirement** in the submission, exactly as M5 declares the target-location term; its P6 re-baseline is the thread detail (post-selection document 2) |
| W8 | Bus voltages and regulated rails | Chosen | **Derived constraint (from G7, 2026-09-18), stated here with its two candidate routes (recorded 2026-09-19).** The machine reaches `E = V_bus` at launch spin — the point of peak load (2.92 kW, C42) — and an alternator delivers into the bus only while `E > V_bus`; a boost stage degenerates at unity gain (D → 0) exactly there. The position is sharper than “no headroom at the peak”: because `E` falls with the decaying body spin (≈1912 → ≈1100 rad/s), the machine’s EMF sits **below** the 400 V bus for most of the trajectory, so the converter is the component that decides whether C42’s energy can be extracted at all. **Two routes, and they are the pair to decide. (1) Keep the bus, raise the EMF headroom, regulate down with a buck charger** — re-cut the winding so `E/ω > V_bus` across the relative-speed envelope. This is the topology the project’s predecessor study chose deliberately, against the same wall and on its own decision record: a low-`Kt` winding whose 150–200 V EMF bucks down to a 54 V supercap bus, keeping the converter controllable at every speed. Its price is arithmetic: at fixed slot area `R ∝ Kt²`, so copper loss rises with the square of the `Kt` increase, and `Kt` is pinned by C14/C19 (0.362 N·m/A rms → 5.76 N·m at 15.9 A). **(2) Keep the winding, lower the bus** so `E > V_bus` at every operating speed — which also improves the partial-discharge and creepage position on which C15/C41 chose the bus in the first place, and lowers the device class the electronics must buy (K5). **The choice belongs to W8 and is taken with C17 (R and L) and the winding.** Either way the stage is **four-quadrant** (W4’s motoring bursts, H8), so the machine-side leg is a buck in one direction and a boost in the other, and the EMF/bus relation decides which regime holds in each. **Closed 2026-09-19 (H12): the bus is set at 180 V nominal — below the flight-minimum EMF (≈ 230 V), with the buck at D ≤ 0.78 at the worst point and ≈ 16.2 A at the 2.92 kW peak — and the rail set is: the machine bus; a machine-side gate-drive rail (H8); the guidance domain's 3.3 V and core rails; the ring's ≈ 0.5–1.5 W rail through H7's transformer; the buffer on the bus (W4); the fuze domain off the reserve supply (W5); and the setter front end on the always-alive rail.** | Derived from G7 (C15 `Kv` 0.209 V/(rad/s) LL, C41 400 V bus, C42 peak load); predecessor-study precedent recorded 2026-09-19 | **DEFINED 2026-09-19 — closed at H12: route A (winding untouched, bus below the flight-minimum EMF), bus 180 V nominal, rail set on the value cell. Converter loading and rail tolerances are P4 outputs.** |
| W9 | Nose aerodynamic heating profile | Derived | | Mach 2+ flight | OPEN |
| W10 | Component temperature limits versus thermal path | Derived | | From W9 | OPEN |
| W11 | Potting compound and thermal conductivity | Chosen | | | OPEN |
| W12 | ⚑ Dump stage specification — element mass, surface area, location, and its share of the nose volume budget | Derived | **DERIVED 2026-09-18 — mass and area follow from W7's path, and neither is a radiator sizing.** Element mass **≈0.15–0.30 kg**, a transient-duty wirewound or film stage. **It is not its own heat sink:** the same 121 kJ dumped into 0.3 kg of element alone would be an **≈800 K rise**, so the element must be **thermally bonded to the stator mount and conduction-coupled to the threads** rather than free-standing — its job is conversion, the structure's job is storage. **Surface area is consequently not the sizing parameter**; the kit-to-shell thermal interface is, at **≥ 58 W/K** peak (≥ 20–27 W/K flight average), **and the bare thread does not deliver it — P6 owns the check and the closing route**. The W4 supercapacitor buffer buffers *transients only* — **it does not reduce the dump's duty**: the 121 kJ integral still leaves as heat through the same interface. **Location: body side, aft of the magnet ring and inboard of the coils**, sharing the stator's thermal path and the shortest conduction route to the threads — so it sits inside the machine's own axial envelope and **does not compete for the nose stack's volume**, which relieves risk 27 rather than loading it. **And it cannot be traded down: C8's 18.1 N requirement sets cant ≥ 10°, and 10° cant is what produces 2.92 kW (C43) — so 121 kJ is the price of the guidance authority the design is built on, not an incidental loss to be trimmed.** This is also the cleanest single sentence separating the de-spun architecture from a servo-actuated canard kit, which has no such load at all. **Annular (2026-09-19): “body side, inboard of the coils” is bounded by F9’s reserved centreline train bore — the dump sits in the annulus with the machine, and the P4 layout must place it there, not on the axis.** | Derived from C42 and W7; the C8/C43 floor; risk 27 for the volume claim | DERIVED — **confirmed by the team 2026-09-18**; element technology and interface conductance fixed in P4 |
